The repo is public, so Miles can fork it himself with nobody's permission, connect his own Vercel to his fork, and can't accidentally push to shared `main`.
> - **`prod-miles` branch exists on origin and is now POINTLESS** — created before the fork decision, nothing deploys from it. **Delete it** (Tyler was asked and hadn't answered before the session ended); it's clutter Brandon will also see.
> - **Setup order matters — two steps must happen BEFORE Miles ever loads the app:** (1) Miles forks from his own account (a fork lands in whoever initiates it, so Tyler can't do it for him); (2) his Vercel connects to **his fork**, not `strategicli/ledgr`; (3) **`npm run db:migrate` against his Neon URL before the first deploy is live** — empty DB + deployed code is a broken instance; (4) **seed his owner row**, or he gets the ADR-184 "Signed in, but not recognized" screen with no nav and it reads as a bug: `SEED_OWNER_EMAIL=<his email> DATABASE_URL=<his neon url> node scripts/seed.mjs`; (5) then he signs in.
> - **Env to boot:** `DATABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `CLERK_SECRET_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_SIGN_IN_URL`. **His own Clerk app** — keys are per-app, sharing Tyler's would put him in Tyler's user pool. Everything else in `.env.example` is optional and degrades quietly; **no R2 means attachments/uploads silently don't work**, which is the one he'll notice. Warn him: since ADR-184 a missing Clerk key on a *deployed* instance is a hard **503**, not a partial app.
> - **The propagation goal ("I push, he gets it") has one real trap: CODE propagates, MIGRATIONS DO NOT.** There's no migrate-on-deploy; the rule is migrate-before-deploy against a specific `DATABASE_URL`. Syncing his fork pushes code, which triggers his Vercel deploy immediately — so **on any change carrying a migration, migrate his DB FIRST, then sync his fork.** Wrong order = his instance serves code his schema can't support.
> - **Two fork constraints to respect when automating:** GitHub disables Actions (especially `schedule`) in forks by default, and **any local commit on his `main` — including a sync-workflow file — breaks fast-forward and turns every sync into a merge**, which is how a fork quietly drifts. **Recommendation: start with the one-click sync** (`gh repo sync milesharrisoncollins-sketch/ledgr --source strategicli/ledgr`), prove his instance works, *then* automate — building automation before the instance exists means debugging two things at once. **The automation shape when wanted:** a workflow that fetches upstream, runs `db:migrate` against his `DATABASE_URL` (a secret in his fork), and **only advances his `main` if the migration succeeded**, which enforces migrate-before-deploy instead of merely documenting it.
> - **Related, now harder to keep deferring: PR #140** (the deploy-workflow proposal — `main` as integration + per-instance prod branches) is exactly this problem, open since 2026-07-01, and a third instance raises the stakes.
> - **⚠️ PARTLY SUPERSEDED 2026-08-14 by ADR-194** (the ✅ entry above). The **fork decision stands** and so does the setup order. What changed is the automation recommendation: the "start with one-click sync, automate later, and don't put a workflow in his fork" advice was right about forks and is now moot, because the automation lives **in the app** instead of in his repo — `/build/updates` lets him take an update himself, and `npm run instances:sync` covers the migration-carrying case from Tyler's side. The migrate-before-sync trap this note identified is now **enforced by the tooling** rather than documented: `instances:sync` refuses to sync a fork whose migration failed, and `LEDGR_SELF_UPDATE=safe` refuses a schema-carrying update in the UI.
>
> **🆕 POLISH — the contact link affordance needs tweaking (Tyler, 2026-08-13; deferred on purpose, detail not yet captured).** Both the phone and email links work on desktop (ADR-192, confirmed above), but Tyler's read is that **"the link button needs some tweaking."** **The specific complaint is not recorded** — this note exists so the observation survives, not because the fix is specified, so don't guess at it: ask before changing anything. Likely candidates, purely as a starting list: the link has no visible affordance until hover (it inherits the cell/row color and only gains `hover:underline`), there's no icon or glyph marking it as callable/mailable the way other actions get one, the whole value is the hit target rather than a deliberate button, and on the record canvas the filled row shows a small icon whose meaning may not read. **Scope note:** if the fix is an icon, it's SVG in the house style, never emoji, and the token layer (`text-ink-*`, not raw grays) — see the standing rules in CLAUDE.md. Two surfaces would need to stay in step: `CustomProperties.tsx` (record canvas) and `ViewRenderer.tsx`'s table cell.

> **✅ BUILT 2026-08-13 (ADR-192) — kept for the record; the ✅ entry above has what actually happened, including where this note's premise was wrong.** ~~🆕 BACKLOG — `phone` and `email` property kinds, so a person's number is tappable on a phone (Tyler, 2026-08-12).~~ Today a phone number or an address on a person goes in a `text` field and renders as dead text: on mobile you read it, then retype it into the dialer. Two new scalar kinds alongside `url` would render as `tel:` / `mailto:` links, which is the whole feature — the OS does the rest (tap to call, tap to mail), and desktop gets a click-to-mail for free. **Why it's a small change with a wide blast radius:** a scalar kind is one entry in `PROPERTY_KINDS` (`src/lib/types.ts:46`) plus every place that switches on a kind — the builder (`build/CustomProperties.tsx`, `TypeBuilder.tsx`, `StructureBuilder.tsx`), the canvas field renderers (`canvas/FieldStrip.tsx`, `lib/canvas-fields.ts`), list/view rendering + filtering (`views/ViewRenderer.tsx`, `lib/views.ts`, `lib/view-where.ts`), input coercion (`lib/item-input.ts`), and `lib/item-tokens.ts` for `{{item.*}}`. `url` is the working reference to copy at each stop. **Validation is the one real decision:** phone formats are a swamp, so store what the user typed verbatim and only normalize for the `href` (strip everything but digits and a leading `+`) rather than rejecting input — a field that refuses a valid number is worse than one that renders an odd one. Email can lean on the same light touch (`type="email"`-ish, warn not block). Touches `schema.md`'s property-kind list, so it's **core** (both-agree + ADR).

> **✅ LONG DONE — the PJ chunk shipped (PR #139, 2026-07-01) and every step below was completed; kept only as the record of how the migration collision was linearized.** ~~⏭️ NEXT (2026-07-01) — DEPLOY THE PJ CHUNK:~~ `integrate-pj` is committed and **merged with `origin/main`** (which had advanced to ADR-137 / PRs→#138: AI Memory, calendar lenses, edit guard, etc.). Migrations linearized past the `0040` collision → `0040_memory_type` then `0041_solid_leech`…`0045_pursuit_type` (whens rewritten so prod won't skip them). tsc + `next build` + eslint clean; verify scripts green on the merged tree. **Remaining, in order (needs prod creds — Tyler):** (1) `npm run db:migrate` against **prod** `DATABASE_URL` (applies the 5 new migrations after `memory_type`) — must precede code going live; (2) push `main` (or PR → merge) → Vercel deploys; (3) verify `/health` + a project page. **Dev-DB gotcha:** `ledgr_dev` already ran the old-numbered branch migrations, so `db:migrate` there will collide — reset dev or fix `__drizzle_migrations` (prod is unaffected, it never had them). Project Type ADR renumbered 133→**138** (origin took 133 for the Planner).

> **🟠 NEW-USER DEAD END — a signed-in user with no `users` row gets a nav-less scaffold (Tyler, 2026-08-06).** Found standing up Tyler's Vercel instance against a fresh database. Two distinct problems, worth separating:

But `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.navPosition` is `"bottom"`, so on a desktop browser it lands as a bottom bar while an existing user expects their left rail — easy to read as "the menu is gone." Open question: should the default be position-aware (rail on desktop, bottom on mobile), and should a brand-new instance seed a couple of type destinations so the nav isn't all built-ins?

Also aborts on a malformed address rather than seeding an unusable owner: the failure mode here is *silent*, so a typo would otherwise cost a debugging session instead of printing an error. Verified against the resolution chain + validator (8 cases, including that an empty string doesn't shadow the fallback); not run against a DB, since it's an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.

## ✅ Recently done — The peer comes back from a bad reboot on its own, and has a face (2026-08-27, ADR-229, not core)

Brandon rebooted the PC and Ledgr did not come back. Three failures stacked: Postgres refused to start because an unclean shutdown left `pg/postmaster.pid` naming a pid Windows had reissued after the boot; nothing was logged, because a Task Scheduler action has no stdout and the supervisor only attached its log files when restarting itself; and no one could restart it, because `supervisor.lock` named pid 4080, Windows had reissued that too, and every check asked only "does that pid exist". `local:status` reported a healthy peer for the whole outage.

Built and verified on the live rig, unmerged and local per the batch-work convention.

- [x] **Identity, not process numbers.** `lockVerdict` takes an `identified` flag read from the running process's command line; aliveness alone never decides. New `supervisor/proc.mjs` does the asking, `lib.mjs` keeps the deciding pure. Unknown identity falls back to whether Postgres is listening, so two supervisors can never share a data directory.
- [x] **A provably stale `postmaster.pid` is cleared automatically,** guarded on the port being free and the pid provably not a postmaster, and worth one fresh round of attempts, once.
- [x] **An orphaned Postgres is stopped cleanly** through the `pg_ctl` path that already existed, instead of losing the port to it forever.
- [x] **Windows runs `ledgr-ctl boot`, not the supervisor** — which attaches the log files and clears a stale lock on the way past. Idempotent, so it is safe from a schedule or a double-click.
- [x] **`process.exitCode` instead of `process.exit()`** in the control script: exiting mid-teardown tripped a libuv assertion and returned 127 after succeeding, which Task Scheduler would have recorded as a failed boot.
- [x] **A notification-area icon** (`npm run local:tray`): green/amber/red, right-click to open, check the ports, start, restart, stop. PowerShell + WinForms, so no new dependency. Every action shells out to the same `ledgr-ctl` verb.
- [x] **Proof, not reasoning.** The outage reproduced deliberately (lock overwritten with a live non-supervisor pid, `postmaster.pid` left stale); `boot` recovered it unattended in 4.4s. The Windows path proved with a throwaway scheduled task: result 0, service still serving after the launcher exited. 22 new pure checks; typecheck, lint, `verify-supervisor`, `verify-setup`, `verify-restart`, `verify-user-guide` all green.

**One thing Brandon must do himself, elevated.** Re-registering the boot task needs an Administrator prompt, so the task still runs the OLD command until then. Until it is re-registered, a reboot behaves exactly as it did on 2026-08-27. In an **Administrator** PowerShell:

```
cd C:\dev\ledgr
npm run local:startup -- --always
```

Use that rather than a hand-typed `schtasks` line. Node passes the argument vector to `schtasks` directly, so the quoting cannot be mangled on the way. **The first attempt at this handed Brandon a `schtasks` command with cmd.exe escaping while telling him to run it in PowerShell, and it failed on paste** — see the finding below; `formatSchtasks` is now shell-aware and the printed fallback carries `--%`.

**Open, deliberately not built:**
- **A watchdog.** Nothing restarts the service if it dies *between* reboots; the tray goes red and offers Start, but nobody is watching a tray at 3am. A second scheduled task running the same idempotent `boot` every 10 minutes would cover it in two lines. Worth deciding on rather than drifting into.
- **The tray on Tyler's instance** is meaningless (his is a cloud deploy) and the verb says so on non-Windows. No action, noted so it is not mistaken for an omission.
- **Email import has been failing since 2026-08-27 17:44**, unrelated to any of this: Microsoft rejects the saved delta token with `410 The sync state generation is not found; generation=292 [highest=295]`. The stored position is older than what Graph still keeps. Fix is to clear that saved position so the next run starts fresh. Not touched here.

## ✅ Recently done — A Restart button that actually reaches healthy (2026-08-26, ADR-227, not core)

Brandon's rule, stated after ADR-225 ended in "now go restart the local service": the GUI should handle 99% of the tasks on a Ledgr install, and if a restart is needed there should be a **button** he can trust to stop, start and come back healthy — whatever the reason, including an update that required it. The manual path then proved the point twice: the `restart` verb he typed did not exist, and the two commands that replaced it left his peer down (a hard kill of mine orphaned Postgres's shared memory, and every retry died on it).

- **The mechanism.** The app cannot restart its own parent, so it writes a request file and the supervisor carries it out — ADR-211's seam — with the twist that the process doing the work is the one leaving: it shuts down cleanly, spawns its successor, and hands over its pid to wait for. **Postgres start now retries** (0/2/5/10s), which is the actual fix for the outage class above.
- **"Healthy" means the port answered**, not that a build pointer exists, and the button proves the restart by a **changed pid** rather than by its own request succeeding. Every phase is on disk, and `handing-off` is the outgoing process's last word so a successor that never started leaves evidence.
- **The thing nobody was saying:** a running supervisor holds the code it started with, so an update touching the supervisor's own files was installed but not in effect. It fingerprints its own source now, and the page says *"An update has arrived that this service predates"* with the button beside it.
- **Two bugs fixed in passing:** the boot-task caveat was a string recorded at registration and never re-checked, so it told Brandon a reboot was not covered when Windows said otherwise; and the boot task registers no working directory, so a boot-started supervisor ran from `System32` (now `chdir`s to its own repo).

**Proven on hardware, twice:** 4.6s and 5.1s from request to a new process reporting healthy, the second run carrying a stale-code case end to end (running `58225c1b` → installed `5a356654` → both matching afterwards), `GET / → 200`, database up. `local:restart` exercised as both a handoff and a cold start. 23 checks in `verify-restart.mts`.

## ✅ Recently done — A job can be assigned from any copy and actually lands there (2026-08-26, ADR-225 + ADR-226, CORE, **Tyler acked**)

Brandon moved the backup, calendar sync and email capture to his always-on PC from the cloud page, got three green indicators, then opened the same page on the PC and read *"wherever it is switched on"* against all three. He asked whether that was how it was supposed to work. It was not, twice over, and measuring the live rig found the second fault was worse than the one he could see.

- **The job was running on neither machine (ADR-225).** Ownership was two switches: the synced slot granted permission, `crons` in `supervisor/config.json` granted the trigger, and exclusive jobs defaulted to off there. So the cloud correctly stood down and the peer was never called. The fix is ADR-222's pattern applied to the three movable jobs: the supervisor schedules them unconditionally and the endpoint's ownership gate is the only switch. An unset slot now resolves per install kind — cloud runs it, supervised peer stands down — so an owner who never opens the picker is exactly where they were, Tyler included. **This closes the follow-up ADR-222 left open** ("the Scheduled jobs section still points at `crons` in supervisor/config.json"), for the jobs the picker can move; the three it refuses to move stay unscheduled on peers, and CI now fails any default-on exclusive job that either cannot be moved or whose route does not call the gate. A stood-down run is recorded as a stand-down, never as work done.
- **The confirm promised a speed the network does not have (ADR-225).** *"Takes effect everywhere within seconds"* is true only between copies syncing continuously; his PC is hourly, and the per-hub cadence gates the exchange in **both** directions, so his own writes were also waiting up to an hour. The dialog now says what happens (this copy stops on confirm, that machine picks it up at its next check-in), names the handover gap, and warns when the target has gone quiet. **"Check in now"** on Build → Network exchanges immediately whatever the schedule says — the lever that was missing entirely, in either direction.
- **And the assignment would have undone itself (ADR-226).** `users.settings` is one jsonb column and the merge is per field, so any settings write carried the writer's whole view and reverted every key it had not yet pulled. The machine holding a job stamps `lastRunAt` into `settings.jobOwners` after each run, so the assigned peer was doing exactly that on a timer, from an hour-old copy of every other setting. Migration `0060` makes the trigger log only the keys that changed and the engine merges them independently. Proven on two real Postgres clusters: cloud names a machine, peer writes an unrelated preference later from a stale copy, both survive both ways.

**Measured, not inferred:** the cloud held all three claims (written 12:30:35–45Z), the peer's pull cursor had last advanced at 12:29:31Z against an hourly cadence, and `ledgr-ctl status` on the peer listed only `purge`, `relatedness`, `snapshot`. **Not yet proven on hardware:** a peer actually running an assigned job after its next check-in, which needs this build on both copies and the local service restarted. **Note for the deploy:** migration `0060` bumps the journal tag, so the two copies will refuse to exchange until both are updated — expected (ADR-206), briefly visible as a version-mismatch notice.

## ✅ Recently done — API credentials: a key ID and a secret you create in the app (2026-08-26, branch `feat/projects-done-and-kanban`, ADR-224, **CORE — agreed with Brandon before merge**)

Issuing an `api`-scoped token used to mean `make-token.mjs`, pasting a hash into `LEDGR_API_TOKENS` on Vercel, and redeploying. Revoking meant a second deploy. So every credential and every revocation cost a production deploy, which is a disincentive to issuing one per caller and a worse one to revoking promptly. `machine.ts`'s own header comment said exactly this, and pointed at the fix already sitting in the repo: `sync_peers` stores token hashes on rows (plan decision 15, revocation is a row flip).

**The credential.** A two-part credential in the Planning Center / Stripe / AWS shape: a **key ID** (`lgrk_…`, public, plaintext, the lookup handle) and a **secret** (`lgrs_…`, hashed with the same `hashToken()`, shown exactly once). Auth is `Authorization: Basic base64(keyId:secret)` as the documented path, so `curl -u` just works, with `Bearer <keyId>:<secret>` accepted for clients that only do bearer. The split earns its keep three ways: lookup becomes one indexed row fetch plus one constant-time compare where the env scheme walks every entry; the UI can finally show *which* credential is which; and the half that leaks into logs and screenshots is the half that grants nothing.

**Both sources resolve, env first.** Two async resolvers in the new `src/lib/auth/credentials.ts` try the unchanged env/stateless check and only then the DB, so `CRON_SECRET` and every existing entry resolve exactly as before and cost no query. The eighteen `/api/machine/*` routes and `/api/mcp` swapped their sync verify call for the async one. The module sits *beside* `machine.ts` rather than inside it, deliberately: `oauth.ts` imports `machine.ts` and commits in its header to being pure `node:crypto`, so putting `getDb()` there would have pulled the database into every OAuth path.

**In User Settings → API credentials:** name it, tick permissions (`api` / `mcp` / `cron` / `diag`, plain-language labels, scope string underneath, **nothing pre-ticked**), submit. The result panel shows key ID and secret with copy buttons, a shown-once warning, and a ready `curl`. The table lists name, key ID, permissions, created, last used, and a per-row Revoke behind the usual ConfirmButton. Revoke stamps `revoked_at`, takes effect on the caller's next request, and touches nothing else. `last_used_at` advances via `after()` off the request path, throttled to a minute; a failure there is swallowed so a usage stamp can never turn a valid credential into a 401. Caps are 25 active and 10/hour, counted in the DB because a serverless instance's memory resets on every cold start.

**`/build/api` is now the reference page**, not the issuing one: what each permission reaches, exact headers, worked examples, and an honest table of every credential path with its revocation story. The ADR-179 app-token minter is folded behind a disclosure rather than removed (defer by hiding), so an app already holding one can be handed a replacement. The CLI + env path stays documented as the deliberate fallback for a credential that must exist before the app can serve a page or must work when the DB does not.

**Verified for real, not argued.** `scripts/verify-api-credentials.mts` is 28 checks against the live dev DB (shape, hash-only storage, both header forms, and every failure closed). Then over HTTP against a running server: 200 on `GET /api/machine/items` via `-u` and via `Bearer key:secret`, 401 on a `cron` route with an `api`-only credential; then revoked from a separate process **with the server never restarted**, and the same call returned 401 — the no-redeploy claim observed. Regression-checked what had to keep working: a fresh CLI env token authenticated a cron route and echoed its identity from `/api/machine/ping`, and a static env `mcp` token completed an `initialize` against `/api/mcp`, as did a minted `mcp` credential.

**Brandon agreed before merge (2026-08-26, relayed by Tyler).** This is CORE. Migration `0059_api_credentials` adds one table (`api_credentials`), additive, nothing existing changed shape. Nothing in it arms on his instance until he creates a credential; every current token, `CRON_SECRET` and the MCP connection keep working untouched. Same rollout note as `0056`/`0057`: the journal tag moves, so sync between peers pauses until each side takes the update. **Not exercised locally:** the OAuth and clipper paths (`LEDGR_OAUTH_SECRET` / `LEDGR_CLIPPER_SECRET` are unset on the dev instance); their code is untouched and still runs ahead of the DB lookup.

## ✅ Recently done — Three surfaces stopped lying, and the config file stopped being a lever (2026-08-25, ADR-222)

Brandon opened what ADR-217 through ADR-221 shipped and found three things that were not so. Nothing here is core: no migration, no wire change, no seam change, and nobody who ignores the new checkbox sees any difference.

**The rule this produced, now a working convention in `CLAUDE.md`: the config file is never the end user's lever.** Every setting a person is expected to change lives in the GUI. `supervisor/config.json`, env vars and JSON blocks are for standing an install up and for testing. If the answer to "how do I turn this on?" is "edit this file and restart the service", the feature has a builder attached and has not really shipped.

- **Restore points are a checkbox now.** ADR-217 put the *keep* number in the app and left the *switch* in `crons.snapshot`. The fix inverts which layer decides: the supervisor schedules `snapshot` hourly on every peer and the endpoint asks `job_state` whether to dump (absent or false → `{ok, skipped}`, recorded as a clean run). Off is still the default; the default just moved into the database. Switching off never deletes what is already on disk. `"crons": {"snapshot": false}` still un-schedules it entirely, as a testing lever.
- **The roster filled a day late, which read as broken.** ADR-220's announce rides `purge` at 03:10 and was the only writer, so a copy that had not run its first purge was missing from its own roster and the "Runs on" picker offered nothing but the two non-machine options. A roster READ now also refreshes this copy's own row when it is absent or older than twelve hours, best-effort. Two extra synced ops a day at worst, which is the constraint that kept announce off the request path.
- **A copy that only RECEIVES was told it syncs with nothing.** `summarizeSync` keyed on `sync.enabled`, which only ever meant "has somewhere to send". A main copy has nowhere: everything pushes into it. So the cloud install opened with *"This device is not syncing with anything"* while a local peer pushed into it every ten seconds. The headline reads both directions now, and "Where your changes go" says the same in its empty state.

**Proven live** against the dev branch: the roster self-heal created this machine's row on a page read and the picker rendered it; the Network page on a peered instance now opens with "2 devices send changes here / It is a main copy" where it used to claim isolation. **Not yet proven on hardware:** an actual hourly dump after the switch is ticked, which needs the local service restarted to pick up the new schedule.


**Follow-on, same day: a local peer can track a release branch (PR #329).** Brandon asked for his local install to auto-update from `prod-brandon` like Vercel does, and doing that on the code as it stood would have been worse than leaving it on `main`. `repoDir` defaults to the checkout the supervisor lives in, so `HEAD` there is whatever branch was last checked out; the update path pulled `origin/<branch>` and then built `rev-parse HEAD`, which only tracked the branch while HEAD sat on it. Pointed at a release branch from a `main` checkout, the pull is a silent no-op and the build takes main's tip — an install serving unreleased code and reporting nothing. The target is `origin/<branch>` now and the pull is gone, so nothing on that path touches or reads the working tree; the auto poll compares against the live build rather than HEAD; and `assembleAppEnv` passes the tracked branch as `GITHUB_BRANCH` so Build → Updates asks about the right ref. Brandon's `supervisor/config.json` is `branch: "prod-brandon"` + `mode: "auto"` (gitignored, so Tyler is untouched). **Known ceiling, deliberately not solved:** the supervisor *script* runs from the checkout rather than from a build worktree, so it cannot self-update from the tracked branch — it runs whatever the clone is sitting on. Fine while releases come from `main`, and the honest fix is a dedicated clone per peer, which is a bigger slice than this warranted.
**One follow-up left deliberately undone.** The *Scheduled jobs on this machine* section still points at `crons` in `supervisor/config.json` for which jobs run at all. That is the same rule as above and wants the same treatment, but moving the whole catalog into the GUI is its own slice rather than a footnote on this one. Snapshots was the one the owner actually needed.

## ✅ Recently done — Two more jobs move, real cadences, and the last of the hub vocabulary (2026-08-25, ADR-221)

Three of the four things `explorations/sync-node-maturity.md` still had open. **Nothing here touches a migration or the wire**, so it is safe beside a peer on an older build, and the only gated piece is §4.

**Job handoffs proven, so `email-import` and `calendar-sync` are claimable.** ADR-218's stated next slice, done. Both blocked reasons were half right and drew the wrong conclusion: the *record* of what has been done is per-machine, but the *answer* is not. Email capture is idempotent because `markImported` moves the message out of the pickup folder (shared, in the mailbox) and `items.properties.email.internetMessageId` syncs; calendar sync cannot duplicate because dedup keys on `items.ms_event_id`, which syncs, and each run pulls the whole window fresh. Each row now states its **consequence** instead of refusing. **The remaining three (`todoist-sync`, `transcription-poll`, `health-check`) stay blocked** and each says why. Proven by two new behavioral runs: `verify-email-in.mts` hands the job over mid-flight against a source that models the real folder (including a created-but-never-moved message), and `verify-calendar-sync.mts` empties `calendar_events` and re-runs. Three pure tripwires in `verify-job-owners.mts` guard the structure the proofs rest on, because a handoff is by definition two databases and every behavioral suite runs against one.

**Cadence is an interval now (§4), CORE-adjacent, Tyler acked.** `HubCadence` is minutes (0 = every normal round); the presets are the ordinary ladder, continuous through weekly. Reads are tolerant, so a hub stored as `"daily"` reads as 1440 and nothing migrates. **The guardrail is the rule "you must be able to miss one sync and still be inside the retention window"**, which lands on weekly against the default 14 days and is why the ladder stops there; longer is refused with the reason rather than clamped. Two things deliberately NOT built, each with its trigger written into the ADR: `grace_days` defaulted from cadence (it lives on the other machine, so it is a wire change, and the cap makes it unnecessary until a preset longer than weekly is wanted) and anchored `{at:"HH:MM"}` (decided against; the real case that would justify it is "only sync overnight", which wants a window).

**§3 vocabulary, closed.** The doc's own resolution, as written: code identifiers unchanged, owner-facing copy drifts to "copy" (the controls ADR-219 left behind), and the wizard asks "Will other devices sync from this machine? (y/n)" instead of "Role (hub or spoke)". `--role hub|spoke` still works verbatim.

**Still owed, and both blocked on PR #327 merging + deploying:** turn snapshots on for one hourly run and browse the copy; claim `export` on the PC and watch the OneDrive backlog drain. Neither is affected by anything above.

**Not picked up:** per-peer boot registration (`STARTUP_TASK_NAME`), still deferred on Brandon's call. It is the last item in the queue at the top of this file.

## ✅ Recently done — The roster: any copy can name any other (2026-08-25, ADR-220, CORE, **Tyler acked**)

The follow-up ADR-218 said would restore the exploration doc's design, built the same day after Brandon weighed four options and picked the expensive one for the right reason: its payoff is much wider than the dropdown it was asked for.

**What it is.** `installs` (migration `0058_installs_roster`) joins the ADR-206 synced set: one row per copy of Ledgr, **keyed by that copy's own `sync_device.id`** — the same id space the scheduler's gate compares against, which is exactly what the old design lacked. Each copy writes only its own row, which is what makes the field-level merge safe and is why this earned a table rather than a key in `users.settings` (that blob is ONE field to the merge, so two copies announcing themselves would clobber each other). Announced daily from `purge`, the one job ADR-214 says every instance runs, so no new scheduler and the cloud joins like a local peer.

**What it bought, beyond the dropdown:** per-copy health ("has not been heard from in over a month") and build visibility on every copy rather than only a hub; a name the owner sets at setup (the wizard now ASKS rather than taking the hostname, which is the collision guard) and can change from any copy; and the fix for a flaw ADR-218 shipped — **a re-fill used to orphan claimed jobs**, because `local:restore` deliberately assigns a fresh `sync_device` id. Both fill paths now carry the identity forward.

**Rollout, and it is not free:** the journal tag moves, so **sync pauses between peers until each takes this update** (same shape as ADR-213/ADR-215).

**Two things found by testing it rather than by reading it:** `announceOwnInstall` resolved the owner as the earliest `users` row, but a database can hold several (a dev branch accumulates throwaway owners from the verify suites) — so the roster row was attributed to a test owner while the page showed an empty list. It resolves the owner like every other scheduled job now, and both roster reads log instead of swallowing, because an empty list and a broken read looked identical. And the duplicate-name warning said "cloud" for machines named "Cloud"; it reports the name as typed now.

**Still open from the exploration doc:** §3 (hub/spoke vocabulary) and §4 (real cadence options) — and the roster is the foundation both of them wanted, so they are cheaper now than when they were written. §4's shape is already decided if it gets picked up: store an interval, keep the presets as UI sugar, and refuse or warn on a cadence longer than the retention window.

## ✅ Recently done — Sync-node maturity §1/§1b/§2 (2026-08-25, ADR-218 + ADR-219)

From `explorations/sync-node-maturity.md`, which is now marked BUILT for those sections. §3 (hub/spoke vocabulary) and §4 (real cadence options) are **still open**, and the options list is untouched except #2, which §1 delivered.

**§1/§1b — one machine per shared job (ADR-218).** Ownership of each EXCLUSIVE job (ADR-214's six) is a single slot in `users.settings.jobOwners`, which syncs, so two owners cannot be represented; every install re-reads it before each run and a machine that lost the job stands down on its next one. Liveness comes from the OWNER STAMPING ITS OWN RUNS, so "set to run on X, last ran 9 days ago" is visible rather than a job quietly not happening. **Absent = the old behavior exactly**, so Tyler's instance and any unconfigured peer are untouched. UI: **Build → Updates → Scheduled work**, one row per job, `Run it here` / `Hand it back` / `Pause it everywhere`.

- **Only `export` is claimable.** The other five each keep their place in the queue in unsynced `job_state`, so a new owner would start from scratch: harmless for some, a silently consumed mailbox for others. Each row states its own reason. **The next slice for anyone picking this up is proving one handoff at a time** — `email-import` idempotency first, then `calendar-sync`'s match cache — and flipping `movable: true` per job in `src/lib/job-owners.ts`.
- **Export's caps lift off the lambda.** 30 items / 45s exist because a Vercel function dies at 60s; on a supervised peer the route passes 500 / 20min. This is what makes moving the job fix the backlog rather than relocate it. **Brandon's live queue was 38 items behind and climbing when this was written** — claiming export on the PC and letting one run happen is the acceptance test.
- **The doc's dropdown could not be built, and this is the fact to carry forward:** `sync_peers.device_id` is minted by the HUB at add-device time and never reconciled with that peer's own `sync_device.id`, so there is no registry holding both id spaces. Claiming is therefore per-machine. **A cross-install device registry would restore the doc's shape and is CORE** (a new synced table or a wire change).

**§2 — the Network page answers first (ADR-219).** One plain sentence at the top with, when needed, the single action that fixes it (`summarizeSync`, 12 pure checks); per-row settings folded behind native `<details>`; a "what is this?" fold per section; "moving to a different main copy" as a numbered flow. The plain-language pass hit what actually leaked ("ops behind", "holding the log", "pull-only", "instance" ~20×) — pleasingly, the worst words ("cursor", "oplog", "cadence", "fallback trust") were never owner-facing at all. **Also fixes the "not due yet is not offline" misreport AT THE SURFACE** (both the headline and the state row); the engine still reports it wrongly and that fix stays queued below as core-adjacent.

## ✅ Recently done — Local snapshots, the "time machine" (2026-08-25, ADR-217)

Point-in-time recovery on a local peer: hourly `pg_dump --format=custom` into `<dataDir>/snapshots/`, thinned into a tiered spread (hourly → 6-hourly → daily → every 3 days) computed from **one** number the owner sets on **Build → Updates → Snapshots** ("restore points to keep", default 30 ≈ three weeks). Fills the gap between `revisions` (one item's body) and the weekly OneDrive dump (exact, but weekly). The dump runs in the app behind `GET /api/machine/snapshot`, triggered by the supervisor's ADR-214 cron seam (`"crons": {"snapshot": true}`, off by default, safe on every peer) — so failures land in `cron-state.json` + `error_log` + `/health` and the job shows up in the existing jobs list with nothing new to build. Config is a `job_state` row (`snapshots:keep`), like the sync-mode override: no config edit, no restart, never synced between peers. `npm run local:snapshot -- list|now|prune|browse <time>`.

**Two things to know before using it.** (1) **Restoring is browse-only, deliberately** — `browse` opens a snapshot in a throwaway cluster on `dbPort + 1000` and there is no `restore` verb (the verify suite fails if one appears), because on an armed peer every write fires the `sync_ops` triggers and an in-place rewind would push weeks-old rows to the hub as fresh edits. In-place restore stays `npm run local:restore`. (2) **`pg_dump` is a real external dependency:** the embedded-postgres packages ship the SERVER ONLY (`postgres`, `initdb`, `pg_ctl` — no client tools at all), so snapshots need `winget install PostgreSQL.PostgreSQL.18`, the same tools the restore-from-file path already needs. Not required on PATH (`C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\*\bin` is checked, as `install.ps1` does); missing tools are reported in the section rather than swallowed.

**A "Snapshot now" button is on the page** (`POST /api/snapshots`, the same `runSnapshot` the hourly job calls). It was cut from the first pass on the grounds that the CLI covers it; Brandon overruled that the same day, and he is right: the moment you want a restore point is the moment you are about to do something *in the app*, and being sent to a terminal right then is how a safety net goes unused.

**Not done, on purpose:** no snapshot browsing *in the UI* (a second app instance against the scratch cluster is a bigger build than it is worth — Claude on the printed connection string does the job); no snapshots on Vercel (no disk, and the route refuses).

**Measured on the rig 2026-08-25 (real numbers, do not re-derive).** The Postgres client tools are now installed on Brandon's PC (`C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin`, not on PATH — the lookup finds it there). A dump of the **live prod-data local hub**: **787 MB database → 300 MB dump in 30 seconds**, against the running cluster with nothing stopped. So the real compression ratio is **0.36**, and `DUMP_COMPRESSION_RATIO` was corrected 0.3 → 0.4 (round up: a high estimate costs a moment's hesitation, a low one costs a full disk). **That makes the default of 30 restore points ≈ 9 GB of disk** — fine on a desktop, and the page states it plainly before you commit, but it is the number to look at when turning snapshots on. Lower the knob if 9 GB is not wanted; 10 buys about a week for ~3 GB.
## ✅ Recently done — Finishing a project: the done checkbox, the board as the front door, and two order bugs (2026-08-25, branch `feat/projects-done-and-kanban`, ADR-223)

Tyler put a project in Done, it stayed on his kanban, and that one report opened four things.

**The done checkbox.** **Mark this project done** in the record header sets the project Done AND completes what's open inside it, over a new `POST /api/items/[id]/complete-project` split into **plan** (GET, read-only) and **apply**. The confirm names real counts before writing ("completes this project and 18 tasks, 3 milestones"); the undo toast holds every item's prior status and reverts verbatim. Scope is CONTAINMENT (home edge, or a `project`/`contains` role), never bare association — completing a neighbour's linked task would be silent damage. Repeating tasks and `statusMode: "none"` types are skipped **and reported** rather than dropped. **No allowlist:** completability is read off each type at plan time, so a type that gains a Done checkbox later is swept from that moment, to its own done key — the rule is the exported pure `sweepDecision`, asserted so nobody narrows it to task/milestone. Unchecking reopens the project only (Tyler's call; the toast is how you take a sweep back).

**The board is the project list's first tab**, and a new **Completed** tab with a search box closes the strip. Both are *synthetic* lens kinds — they build a ViewDefinition from the type instead of pointing at a saved view, which is what lets a type ship a kanban as its default. Terminal columns collapse to a rail by default but stay counted, expandable and **droppable**, so dragging onto collapsed Done completes a project. Mobile: full-width snap-scrolled columns, snapping dropped mid-drag so it can't fight the touch-drag auto-scroll.

**Two order bugs, not one.** The lens path never computed a status board's `groupOrder` (so columns fell to their ALPHABETICAL tail — Done first, `STATUS_7` as a header). Then, *found only by rendering a real board*, all four call sites built the order as `statuses.map(s => s.key)` — but that is the AUTHORED order; `orderedStatuses` is what sorts by category. On Tyler's schema that put Done fifth of seven, mid-strip. Both fixed everywhere, both now asserted in `verify-project-done.mts` (22/22, pure, in CI) — the first draft of that script sailed through the second bug because it fed itself `orderedStatuses(...)` by hand.

**The vanished descriptions were never lost** (verified by reading a body out of production). The 2026-07-01 redesign dropped `overview` from the default project layout and the canvas only rendered a description when the layout carried that section. Now: render it when the layout asks OR when the body has content and the owner hasn't explicitly hidden it — self-healing, no migration. Statuses also gained ↑/↓ reordering within a category in Build.

**Left for Tyler:** the apply + undo write path is the one thing not exercised against a database (ADR-206 sync could propagate a local write) — click it on "This is a Test project" and confirm the toast's undo restores everything. ~~And **`verify-provider-seams.mts` fails on `main`**~~ — **fixed 2026-08-26** as a drive-by in ADR-224: `NavAuthHeal` now reads the browser's session through a new seam file (`src/lib/auth/client.ts`, `useClientSession()`) instead of importing Clerk directly, so Clerk stays confined to seam files and `npm run ci` is green again.

## ✅ Recently done — No-nav-after-sign-in fixed (2026-08-24, branch `fix/stale-chrome-after-sign-in`, ADR-216)

Every login landed on Today with tasks but zero nav until a full page load (Tyler, Safari, "literally every time"). Cause: the login flow's last server render is genuinely signed out; Clerk finishes the session client-side and soft-navigates, and the App Router keeps the stale signed-out root layout (Clerk's own compensating `router.refresh()` races its navigation and drops — aggravated by the root `loading.tsx`, vercel/next.js#86151). Fix: `NavAuthHeal` (`src/components/nav/NavAuthHeal.tsx`), mounted only by signed-out Nav renders, fires one throttled `router.refresh()` when the client-side Clerk session disagrees. Parked follow-ups: (a) bump `@clerk/nextjs` 7.5.2 → 7.8.x as routine maintenance (may fix the upstream race; the heal stays as the guarantee either way); (b) if the ~0.5s nav pop-in on landing ever bothers, the crisper escalation is a hard-redirect bounce page at the sign-in fallback URL (weigh against the `redirect_url` flows first — see ADR-216's rejected list).
## ✅ Recently done — Local hub/spoke LH4: the one-file installer + setup wizard (2026-08-22, branch `local-hub-installer`)

A brand-new Windows machine goes from one downloaded file to a working Ledgr peer. **`install.ps1`** (PowerShell 5.1-safe, idempotent, no secrets, no self-elevation): winget-installs git/Node LTS with manual-download fallbacks, clones into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Ledgr\app` (or `-InstallDir`), `npm ci`, hands off to **`npm run local:setup`**, the cross-platform wizard (node builtins only; every prompt has a flag override so it runs unattended). Wizard flow: preflight → hub-or-spoke → data dir/ports → owner email → spoke hub URL + one-time device token (redacted in all output) → data fill (restore via `local-restore.mjs`, or migrate+seed for start-empty, or skip) → config written through the supervisor's own `normalizeConfig`, never clobbered without `--force` → service registration (exact `schtasks /F` on win32, launchd/systemd instructions printed elsewhere) → summary. Enabling edit: `scripts/seed.mjs` gained the same node-postgres driver branch as migrate.mjs so start-empty can seed a local cluster. First build stays the supervisor's job (LH2). Unattended smoke on this Mac proved the full hub path against a real embedded-postgres scratch cluster (initdb → 54 migrations → seed with owner row → clean shutdown), the no-`--force` refusal, and the spoke re-config. verify-setup 53/53; full verify:ci 66/66. User guide deliberately unchanged (nothing new in-app; ADR-189's test says no entry). `install.sh` deferred post-cutover; the wizard is already platform-neutral and proven on darwin. **Field-revised the same day from Brandon's first hub install (ADR-206 addendum 2):** `install.cmd` is now the file you download and double-click (Explorer opens a `.ps1` in an editor rather than running it, which stopped attempt one), and initial data offers three sources instead of one: a backup dump file, a **live database pull** (`--from-url <direct connection string>`, fresher and less for the first sync to reconcile), or start empty. The connection string is never printed, stored, or retained, `pg_dump` stderr is redacted, the temp dump is unlinked in a `finally`, and a `-pooler` hostname is refused before any network call (the deliberate inverse of `assertPooledUrl`, commented at both sites).

## ✅ Recently done — Local hub/spoke LH3 code half: the sync surfaces (2026-08-22, branch `local-hub-alongside`)

Everything user-facing the alongside phase needs. **Synced devices** on `/build/updates` (plan decision 15): add a device (32-byte token shown exactly once, sha256 hash stored on `sync_peers`), last seen, "n ops behind" cursor lag, Revoke/Restore/Delete (delete only when revoked), all owner-authed under `/api/sync/peers*` (machine auth stays only on `/api/machine/sync`). **SyncPill**: a nav dot on local peers only (server-side env gate, cloud/Tyler render nothing), green/amber/red with a CSS-hover tooltip, click through to `/build/updates`; a **Sync section** there shows state, primary/backup hub, pending ops, last sync, last error. `gatherSyncStatus()` computes pendingOps on demand from the oplog vs the hub cursor so a stale in-memory "synced" flips to pending between loop runs. **`/health`** now reports `schema` (the previously unwired `getSchemaStatus()`) and `sync`. User guide updated (ADR-189). verify-sync-ui 20/20; full verify:ci 65/65; browser-verified end to end in dev-auth preview (device lifecycle, nav dot in rail + mobile, two real layout bugs found and fixed). Dev Neon branch migrated to 0054 in passing (it was behind, the standing dev-DB-currency issue).

## ✅ Recently done — Local hub/spoke LH2: the local runtime (2026-08-22, branch `local-hub-runtime`)

Ledgr now runs fully local, supervised, and self-updating. **`supervisor/ledgr-supervisor.mjs`** (node builtins only): starts embedded Postgres (initdb on first run) + `next start` from the live build, crash restarts with capped backoff, graceful shutdown. **Updates, the local half of ADR-194:** the `/build/updates` "Update now" button on a local peer writes a signal file (`ApplyStrategy: "supervisor-signal"`, gated on non-Vercel + `LEDGR_SUPERVISOR_DIR`, fail-closed); the supervisor then does `git pull` → npm ci only if the lockfile changed (else copies node_modules from the live build; Turbopack rejects symlinks) → `next build` into a fresh self-contained worktree at `builds/<sha>/` → migrate → flip the `live.json` pointer → restart. Any failure leaves the previous build serving; prune keeps 2 builds. **`localAuthProvider`** (ADR-206 decision 5): `LEDGR_LOCAL_OWNER_EMAIL` + no Clerk + not a deployed Vercel env → fixed identity "local-owner" resolved by email; the selection rule is a pure `chooseAuthProvider` in `src/lib/auth/local.ts` under test, and ADR-184's fail-closed posture is untouched (a deploy missing its Clerk key still 503s). **`npm run local:restore`**: pg_restore of the weekly backup into the embedded cluster, migrate to the bundled journal, then clear cloned sync state (truncate sync_ops, re-seed sync_device, truncate sync_peers, delete `sync:cursor:%` job_state rows). **Proven on this Mac end to end:** real supervisor run (initdb → build → serve, `/health` 200 on the embedded cluster), a real signal-file update into a new sha, and a deliberately broken commit that left the old build serving (keep-last-good proven, not assumed). **Windows bring-up checklist** in `supervisor/README.md` (§1l pointer in runbook). verify-supervisor 57/57; verify-sync/provider-seams/updates green; full verify:ci 64/64. Next: LH3 (queued above).

## ✅ Recently done — Local hub/spoke LH0+LH1: ADR-206 + the sync spine (2026-08-22, branch `local-hub-sync-spine`)

The local build is decided (ADR-206, Tyler agreed verbally; 15 landed decisions; built alongside Vercel+Neon with cutover on Brandon's call) and phase 1 shipped in one slice. **Migration `0054_sync_spine`:** `sync_device` (self-assigned one-row identity), `sync_ops` (the oplog: per-device bigserial seq, `changed` jsonb diffed in-trigger, `schema_ver` stamp, nullable `origin_device_id` for echo containment), `sync_peers` (device registry: token hash, revoke flag, cursors), plus one `sync_log_op()` trigger function and AFTER-row triggers on the 8 synced tables (users fires on settings changes only; UPDATE triggers guarded `WHEN (OLD.* IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.*)` so no-op writes log nothing, which is what terminates echo loops). **Code:** `src/lib/sync/engine.ts` (pure merge: per-field LWW by `(at, device_id)`, losing body → `revisions` + a `properties.syncBodyMerged` flag, relations as set ops, double-apply is a no-op), `apply.ts` (transaction + `ledgr.sync_origin` GUC on node-postgres; echo-termination on neon-http), `auth.ts` (`verifySyncDevice`, sha256 + timingSafeEqual against `sync_peers`), `client.ts` (ordered-hub-list walker, push ~2s / pull ~10s, armed only by `LEDGR_SYNC_HUBS` via `src/instrumentation.ts`), `/api/machine/sync` (device auth, schema-version gate 409s on journal-tag mismatch, 500-op batches). **Driver branch:** non-Neon `DATABASE_URL` → node-postgres (`pg`, eval-required so client bundles never trace it); Neon path byte-identical; `dbSupportsTransactions()`. **Verified:** `verify-sync.mts` 36/36 including a real two-database convergence battle on ephemeral `embedded-postgres` clusters (same-field and same-body offline conflicts, hard-delete propagation, echo termination, owner-scope + version-gate refusals); `verify-provider-seams` updated for `verifySyncDevice` and green; typecheck/lint/build/full `verify:ci` green. Nothing user-facing yet (guide untouched by design); no DB was migrated (migrations apply per-instance, as always). Next: LH2, the local runtime (queued above).

Tyler's second decision wave from the preview session, all four built same-session. `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean, 59 pure suites green. **NOT browser-verified** — the preview click-through is the test.

Default quiet window 7→14d; per-project **Check-ins** control (off switch + window) beside Add a Tool; quiet active projects surface atop **Tasks → Today** as P1-styled virtual "Check on …" rows that dissolve when the project is opened. `checkin_reviewed` hidden from the Recent Activity card (plumbing, not narrative).
- **⏭️ Still parked from the idea wave:** (1) **favorites float-to-top** (needs a query-side boost to be honest across pagination); (2) **notes organization / folders on a project** (`explorations/notes-organization.md`; notes already nest via `parent_id`, the Docs surfaces just render flat — custom-type tools cover the heavier case now); (3) **"Meetings" vs "Events" card label** (keep the milestone/meeting split; the label is a one-liner if wanted); (4) custom-tool cards in the MCP `set_type_layout` show-vocabulary (`recordWidgets()` in mcp/tools/records.ts doesn't offer synthetic ids yet — additive, solo).
