## ✅ Recently done — Tyler's instance now migrates on build (2026-08-16, instance ops, not core)

**The ADR-194 schema-drift scenario bit Tyler's instance two days after the warning shipped.** Brandon's Listen release (PRs #259/#260) carried migration `0050_small_joystick` (two additive `types` columns). Tyler's Vercel project auto-deployed the code from `main`, but nothing ran the migration against `ledgr_prod` (Neon `savor-db` project), so Drizzle's `SELECT`s asked for `listen_enabled` on a table that didn't have it — every `types` query 500'd, which is nearly every page. Symptom on the installed PWA: "page couldn't load" at launch. Tyler rolled production back to `8e0b014` (last pre-Listen commit) and the app came back, which confirmed the diagnosis.

- **The fix that stays fixed:** the `tylerjaycollins-projects/ledgr` **Build Command is now `npm run build:satellite`** (`node scripts/migrate.mjs && next build`), set via the project-settings API on 2026-08-16 — **deliberately NOT via `vercel.json`**, which is shared with Brandon's project and would have silently flipped his instance to migrate-on-build too. A failing migration fails the build, and Vercel keeps the previous working deploy live, so the failure mode is "didn't get the feature yet," never "app is down."
- **Migration 0050 itself lands via the first satellite build** (redeploy of the latest `main` deployment), through Drizzle's own migrator — no hand-run SQL, so the `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` journal stays honest.
- **Why Brandon never hits this:** he migrates before merging (his DB, his rhythm). Tyler receives releases passively, so his pipeline had automated code and manual schema — this closes that gap. `/build/updates` (ADR-194) remains the dashboard; with migrate-on-build its schema check should now always read current.
- **Open thread:** after a Vercel rollback, new production deployments may not auto-promote until promoted once; verify the redeploy actually took the `ledgr-sandy.vercel.app` alias. And if the "menu again on relaunch" symptom survives the healthy deploy, report it as its own bug — during the outage it was presumed downstream of the 500s.

## ✅ Recently done — The offline page lists what you pinned (2026-08-14, branch `offline-landing-page`, ADR-193, not core)

**Save Offline pinned fine and was unreachable.** The pin has worked since ADR-019, but there was no path to a pinned item except already knowing its URL, and the PWA's `start_url` (`/`) is not pinned — so cold-launching in airplane mode hit `public/offline.html`, a static "You're offline / Try again" dead end. Pin Sunday's sermon Saturday night, and Sunday morning you had nothing to click.

- **`public/offline.html` is now the directory,** listing every pin on that device, most recently saved first, each row linking to its pinned copy. **The service worker's routing did not change and did not need to:** a static page has no Next router, so every link on it is a hard navigation, which lands on the existing `request.mode === "navigate"` branch where the pin fallback already lives.
- **No registry, and that was the whole design win.** The list comes from `ledgr-pin-v1` alone: `cache.keys()` filtered to `/^\/items\/([^/]+)\/print$/` (one regex that also collapses the bare `/items/{id}` twin *and* skips pinned images), titles scraped from the `<title>` `print-html.ts` already writes. A `localStorage` shadow was rejected as a second store that can drift from the pins it describes, and can outlive them.
- **Rows sort on the saved stamp, not cache key order** (Brandon's call mid-build, and he was right). `SaveOffline` stamps a `Date` header; insertion order was a free and correct-today proxy, but a re-save `cache.put`s over the same key, so only an explicit sort guarantees that row returns to the top. Pins predating the header sort last with no date shown.
- **⚠️ The `VERSION` bump in `sw.js` (v8 → v9) is part of the change, not hygiene.** `offline.html` is precached and served cache-first, so editing it *without* bumping leaves every installed device on the old copy, silently, with nothing to debug. Any future `PRECACHE` edit owes the same bump; `verify-offline-landing` asserts `VERSION > v8`.
- **The SPA-navigation worry was real and self-healing, so it's deliberately NOT fixed.** Read out of the installed Next 16.2.9 source: a `<Link>` tap fetches `/items/{id}?_rsc=…`, which isn't `mode: "navigate"`, so it misses in the catch-all branch (no `ignoreSearch`, and the cache-buster defeats the match). Next then catches the failure and does an MPA navigation, and *that* hard load hits the navigate branch and serves the pin. **Do not add `ignoreSearch: true` to the catch-all** — it would let pinned HTML answer API and RSC requests that asked for something else.
- **Free, and in the user guide:** `/offline.html` is a static file Next serves, so it renders the same live list **online**. That's the Saturday-night "did my pins land?" check at zero code cost.
- **Verified in-browser, not just structurally:** pins seeded deliberately out of date order still render newest-first; re-saving the oldest moved it to the top with today's date and no duplicate row; the bare-`/items/{id}` twins and a pinned image never appear as rows; a pin with no `Date` header sorts last and renders without a date instead of breaking; empty state reads as help, not a broken list; zero console errors, desktop and mobile widths.
- `npm run ci` green (tsc, eslint, `next build`, **56 pure suites** incl. new `scripts/verify-offline-landing.mts`, 20 checks — it reads the key regex back out of `offline.html` so it can't drift from what ships).
- **⚠️ NOT yet verified on real hardware, and it cannot be locally.** `sw.js`'s `IS_DEV` covers `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`*.local` and makes the worker a self-unregistering passthrough (the v6 reload-loop fix), so a local `next build && next start` proves nothing about it. The browser pass above exercised the page's own logic against a real Cache API, which is a different claim from the worker serving it. **Owed: on a deploy, DevTools-offline hard-reload `/`, then real airplane mode cold-launching the installed PWA.** Note the change only reaches a device after **one online launch** post-deploy, when the v9 worker installs.

## ✅ Recently done — A user guide, held once and served three ways (2026-08-12, ADR-189)

**The problem was Tyler asking for features he already had** — which was never a Tyler problem. Nothing in Ledgr tells you what Ledgr does: CLAUDE.md says how it's built, the PRD says what was intended, the runbook says how to operate it, and 184 ADRs say why. None of them answer "what can I do, and where is it." At 184 ADRs, that gap is most of the product.

- **One source:** `src/lib/mcp/user-guide.ts`, a markdown constant in a `.ts` wrapper — the pattern `guide.ts` already uses, so no `fs`, no bundler config, no tracing rules, and it behaves the same in the serverless MCP route and a server component.
- **Three doorways:** the MCP resource `ledgr://guide/using-ledgr` (ungated — no owner data), the page at `/build/guide` under MAINTAIN, and links from the Work "More" menu and the command palette. All three verified in a real browser.
- **A feature index, not a tutorial.** Every entry names a capability and its route, because the failure mode was *not knowing a thing exists*. It closes with a **"Not here yet"** section (notifications paused, Hygiene/Import stubs, no light theme, Todoist off, no footnotes outside Papers) so nobody hunts for vapor.
- **Written from a verified sweep,** not from the ADR log alone: four parallel inventories over the route tree, the 184 ADRs, the editor/body dialect, and the integration + MCP surface, each cross-checked against code. That's what kept the paused and stubbed things out of it.
- **Two small shared changes:** `BuildEntry` and the palette's destination result grew an optional `keywords` array (so "help" and "docs" reach an entry called "User Guide"), and the page imports `markdown-editor.css` directly because `.ledgr-prose` ships with the lazy editor bundle.
- **Upkeep is a process rule, not automation:** CLAUDE.md's "When you finish a slice" and the `/ship` gate both ask whether the slice changed what the owner can do. Nothing generates the guide from code, on purpose.
- `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean; `scripts/verify-user-guide.mts` (new) is green, and it fails if the guide ever cites a `/build/…` route that doesn't exist.

> **🟡 FOLLOW-UP — the body-dialect contract in CLAUDE.md overstates what's built.** Found during the ADR-189 sweep. The "Editor & body format" bullet lists "Pandoc features — footnotes, superscripts, citations, attribute spans" as part of the canonical dialect, and **no code produces or renders any of the four**: `markdown-render.ts` runs markdown-it with no footnote plugin, and the shared editor has no extension for them. Footnotes work only inside the **Papers module**, which uses a plain textarea precisely because the WYSIWYG editor can't handle them. That line is the dialect's *written contract* — CLAUDE.md says adding to it is a core change — so a contract that overstates reality is misleading in the one authoritative place. **Correcting it is itself core (both-agree + ADR), so it was flagged, not edited.** Options: describe what's built, or keep the ambition and mark those four as not-yet-implemented. Raised with Tyler in `COLLAB.md`.

> **🟡 FOLLOW-UP — `verify-build-mode.mts` has one stale assertion, red on `main`.** Noticed while merging ADR-189, **pre-existing and not caused by it**: line 49 asserts `isBuildPath("/dashboards/abc")` is true, but `isBuildPath` deliberately returns **false** for an individual dashboard, and the comment above it in `build-nav.ts` says why — `/dashboards/<id>` is the "using it" context and keeps Work chrome, and *"claiming `/dashboards/...` as Build here is what once made Build mode the only way to view an unassigned dashboard."* The code is right and the test predates that fix. Not in the `release:prod` gate (that runs `verify-db`, `verify-items`, `verify-relations`, `verify-types`), so nothing is blocked — but a suite that always reports one FAIL is one people stop reading, which is exactly the ADR-184 `verify-provider-seams` complaint two entries down. One-line fix: assert false, and keep the `/dashboards` (index) and `/dashboardsfoo` cases as they are.

> **✅ FIXED — the command palette 404'd on template results.** Found during the ADR-189 route sweep, pre-existing and unrelated: the palette emitted `/build/templates/{id}/edit`, a route that never existed. Fixed the same day in PR #248: a template has no editor of its own (it *is* an item, ADR-093), so the palette now opens its prototype at `/items/<prototypeItemId>`, the same target the templates index uses, and `verify-build-mode.mts` pins the href so it can't come back.

## ✅ Recently done — A missing auth key fails closed, and an unresolvable session says so (2026-08-11, ADR-184, auth seam)

**Reported as "my user menu disappears sometimes."** It wasn't a nav bug — the ADR-182 nav commit that had just shipped never touches the More kebab. Tyler's own read was right: *"maybe I'm logged in but not really?"*

- **The cause:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is read in **two** places that failed in **opposite** directions when absent. `src/proxy.ts` fell back to a pass-through — **nothing protected** — while `src/lib/auth/index.ts` fell back to `nullAuthProvider` — **everyone signed out**. Door open, building believed empty. Then `resolveOwner()` → null → `Nav.tsx`'s `if (!owner) return null` → the **whole nav vanishes** while page bodies render fine. A production fail-open whose only symptom is a missing menu.
- **Fail closed:** new `src/lib/auth/keyless.ts` keys "is keyless allowed here" on `VERCEL_ENV` (set on any deployment, unset locally and in CI — exactly the split the fallback wants). Missing key + deployed → **503** + one JSON log line per blocked request. Public routes still pass (`/api/machine/*`, `/api/mcp`, Todoist webhook, `/share`, `/api/ics` all authenticate themselves), so a missing key doesn't also break cron, MCP, and the ICS feed; `/health` stays up as the diagnostic.
- **Name it, show it:** `resolveOwnerState()` returns `signed-out | unrecognized | owner` and warns with the failing identity; the home route says so in words. `resolveOwner()` keeps its `Owner | null` signature, so ~80 callers are untouched.
- **Diagnostic worth remembering** (also in runbook §7): a nav-less page, tested in one load — **fresh incognito**. Straight in with no login prompt and no nav = the key is missing. Bounced to `/sign-in` = the key is fine, it's the owner row.
- `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean; `verify-provider-seams` still passes all four auth checks (`keyless.ts` imports nothing, so it doesn't become a fifth Clerk seam file).

> **🟡 FOLLOW-UP — teach `verify-provider-seams` the second machine-token helper.** Noticed while verifying ADR-184, **pre-existing and unrelated**: the script reports `FAIL every /api/machine endpoint verifies a machine token` for `attachments`, `capture`, `items`, and `relations`. Those routes **are** gated — they use `verifyApiToken()` + `resolveMachineOwner` (the ADR-117 OAuth shim, `db6ae4b`), while the guard greps only for `verifyMachineToken(` (`scripts/verify-provider-seams.mts:74`). Not a security hole, but a security guard that cries wolf on every run is one people learn to ignore. One-line fix: accept either helper.

> **🟡 FOLLOW-UP — the ~80 page-level null-owner paths (deferred from ADR-184).** Pages call `resolveOwner()` directly and each decides what a `null` means: `page.tsx` renders a hero, `Nav.tsx` renders nothing, others vary. API routes are consistent (`requireOwner()` 401s, `src/lib/api.ts`), pages aren't. A `requireOwnerPage()` that redirects or renders the unrecognized state once would make the whole surface behave alike — but it's a large mechanical diff across ~80 files and deliberately didn't ride along on an incident fix. Worth doing on its own branch, with the loop hazard in mind: **an `unrecognized` session must not redirect to `/sign-in`.**

## ✅ Recently done — One Search icon, and you pick what it opens (2026-08-11, ADR-182, non-core)

**From Tyler: two search surfaces both appeared automatically, the popup one was "baked in rather than being a user choice," and it "appears darker than the other icons."** All three were accurate and all three had **one** cause: `/search` was seeded into `DEFAULT_NAV_SLOTS` *and* a permanent palette button was hardcoded into **all four** nav layouts (desktop pill, mobile bar, top bar, side rail). Two search icons by default, only one configurable — and the hardcoded one used `text-neutral-500` while slots beside it on a rail/top bar render at `text-neutral-400`. **That one tone is the "darker" icon.**

- **One Search destination + `searchMode: "palette" | "page"`** (default `palette`), set on **Build → Navigation** above the slots editor. The four hardcoded buttons are **deleted**, so Search is a slot like any other — which fixes the color **by construction**, since it now takes its class from the same per-layout builder as its neighbors and can't drift again.
- **⌘K opens the palette in both modes.** A shortcut costs no bar space, so picking "page" never strands the popup — that answers the old ADR-172 follow-up note, which had rejected special-casing `/search` precisely because it feared making the page unreachable. The answer was a setting, not two icons.
- **The non-obvious part:** `launcherTiles` flat-maps nav slots into plain `<Link>` tiles for the mobile drawer, and a tile only shows when the slot *isn't* in the bar. So an owner whose Search slot overflowed would get a drawer tile that quietly did the **other** thing. `LauncherTile` gained an optional `onSelect`; such a tile renders as a button and closes the drawer first. Found by counting affordances in the served HTML, not by reading.
- Also additive on MCP: `searchMode` on `update_nav` + `navView`, so the model can read and set it.
- **Verified live in both modes** against the real dev owner's customized nav: `palette` → one "Search (⌘K)" button, **zero** `/search` links (bar slot *and* drawer tile both open the overlay); `page` → zero palette buttons, two `/search` links (both navigate). Reachable either way, never doubled. Setting returned to its default after. `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean; `verify-mcp` ALL PASS.

## ✅ Recently done — MCP shapes projects end to end + status terms are findable in Build (2026-08-11, ADR-181, CORE, additive)

**From Tyler, three asks across the session.** (1) Projects should let the user define their status terms in Build. (2) MCP should shape projects end to end: pick what tools are shown, add things to each tool, add tasks/notes to a project. (3) The **type-level editor** too, so the sections and the status terms can both be changed by hand.

- **Ask 1 was already built and shipped unchanged.** The "Completion tracking" panel (`StatusSchemaEditor`, ADR-106/082) renders on `/build/types/[key]/edit` for **every** type, and `project` already carried five terms (Ongoing / Waiting for Others / Paused / Future / Done) with label, color, per-category default, add and remove. Verified live at 200 with all five in editable inputs. **The gap was findability:** nothing pointed at the panel, so "where do I rename my project stages?" had no visible answer from the type index. One badge per Build → Types row now says "5 statuses" / "done checkbox". The editor was deliberately not rebuilt.
- **Ask 2 was real, and half of it was missing from the whole app.** `types.default_widgets` (Layer 2 — what every record of a type shows) has been **read** on the render path since PJ3 with **no writer anywhere**: no lib function, no route, no UI. Only Layer 3 (one record, via "+ Add section") was editable. `setTypeDefaultWidgets` is that writer.
- **Five new MCP tools:** `get_record_layout` (what shows / hidden / addable, and whether it comes from the record, the type, or the built-in default), `set_record_layout`, `set_type_layout`, `add_to_record` (create one or many tasks/notes/milestones/events/links inside a record, or file an existing item in — sets HOME), `set_type_statuses`. `list_types` now reports `statusMode` + the effective terms.
- **show / hide / order / options are ops, not a whole-composition PUT** — a model resending a full array would drop what it forgot (the trap `update_type`'s full-`propertySchema` replace already has). One pure `applyLayoutOps` serves both layers so they can't drift. Options are validated against each widget's own declared schema.
- **A bug the verify script caught:** the mode-only path of `set_type_statuses` wiped a type's custom terms, because a null schema in *select* mode means the explicit "inherit the default" choice. Fixed by resending the stored schema; `inherit: true` is the deliberate clear.
- **⚠️ ADR-180 left `verify-mcp.mts` failing** — its tool list is exhaustive (`length === EXPECTED.length`) and that slice ran its own new script, tsc, eslint and the build but not this pre-existing suite. Both slices' tools are now in it and it's back to ALL PASS. **Worth a habit change: run the whole `verify-mcp*` set when touching the MCP registry.**
- **Verified:** `scripts/verify-mcp-records.mts` (new, **91 checks, ALL PASS**) through the real dispatcher against live Neon under throwaway owners **and a throwaway type**, asserting the real `project` type is only read. Plus **live in a browser**: an MCP-shaped project rendered with Meetings/Milestones gone, Timeline added, and "Showing 1 of 2 →" proving the `limit` option landed. `verify-mcp` + `verify-mcp-tasks` ALL PASS; `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean.
- **Built too (Tyler asked right after the MCP half): the type-level editor in Build.** `TypeSectionsEditor` — a **"Record sections"** panel sitting immediately after **"Completion tracking"** on `/build/types/[key]/edit`, so *which sections records show* and *what the status terms are called* are both manually editable in one place. Checkbox per section, ↑/↓ to reorder, a "show N rows" count on the cards that preview, Save, and "Reset to built-in". Writes through a focused route (`PATCH /api/types/[key]/widgets`) like `/statuses` and `/layout`, so it can't clobber a builder edit.
  - It **seeds from the effective layout**, not a blank slate, so a first save copies current behavior plus the one change you made.
  - It writes every section with `hidden: true` on the off ones, so re-enabling restores position + options, and the **Build door and the MCP door store the same shape**.
  - `behaviors` (digest settings) carry through untouched. Reorder is buttons, not a drag dependency (Principle 5).
  - **Verified live:** both panels render on the project type page with the sections in real order; a PATCH round-tripped hidden + `limit` + digest; a new project then read `source: "type"` and rendered exactly the saved sections; reset returned `project.default_widgets` to null (left as found).

## ✅ Recently done — MCP can do subtasks and recurring tasks (2026-08-11, ADR-180, CORE, additive)

**From Tyler: "the MCP needs to be able to do Subtasks, and Recurring tasks as well. Currently the MCP cannot do either."** Both were true, and both were exposure gaps only: nesting has existed since slice 7 and native recurrence since ADR-073/076/083/085. `buildWriteRaw`'s `WRITE_FIELDS` whitelist just omitted `parentId` (so create/update silently dropped it), and the only path to a repeat rule was hand-writing `properties.recurrence` — asking the model to compose an RRULE blind. No new machinery: four tools wrapping the libs the REST routes already call.

- **`list_subtasks`** (nested tree + the "n of m done" rollup, task children only), **`add_subtasks`** (the "break this down" call — mixed title strings and objects, **validated all-or-nothing** so a bad last entry can't leave half a checklist), **`set_recurrence`** (natural-language `repeat` through the same `nl-date.ts` parser quick-add uses, or structured parts, or a partial edit of an existing rule), **`update_occurrence`** (`complete`/`uncomplete`/`carve` on one date — **idempotent, not a toggle**, because a wrapped toggle is a trap for a retrying model).
- Plus `parentId` + `scheduledDate` on `create_item`/`update_item`, and a `recurrence` block on `get_item` that's **absent entirely** for a non-recurring item so the old shape is byte-for-byte intact.
- **Two real bugs found by the verify script, not just bad expectations.** (1) `rowView` never returned `scheduledDate` at all — the planned date was invisible across the *whole* MCP surface (search, list, get, create, update). Now included. (2) `"monthly on the 3rd"` degrades in `nl-date.ts` to a plain `FREQ=MONTHLY` with **no error to notice** (the parser's spelling is `"the 3rd of the month"`); normalized MCP-side rather than widening the shared parser, so quick-add's behavior is untouched.
- **One deliberate divergence from the canvas control:** editing a rule here **keeps** the completion log (`resetLog: true` opts out), where `RecurrenceControl` wipes it via `makeRecurrence`. A model tweaking `until` shouldn't silently discard months of stamps. The UI's wipe is arguably a latent bug, left alone.
- **Verified:** `scripts/verify-mcp-tasks.mts` (new, **90 checks, ALL PASS**) drives the real `callTool` dispatcher against live Neon under throwaway owners — including both cycle guards, two all-or-nothing cases creating nothing, seven NL phrases asserted to exact RRULE + description, complete/uncomplete idempotence both ways, carve, and owner scoping on all four tools. `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean.
- **Owed:** a COLLAB heads-up to Brandon (done) — this is the **CORE MCP contract**, so it wants his ack even though it's additive-only and his instance is unchanged until he deploys.

## ✅ Recently done — API Tokens: a findable, named token for an external app (2026-08-08, ADR-179, CORE)

**From Tyler: "we made an API token generator, but I'm not seeing it now. I generated a token for a new project called Overtone and need to hand it to the app."** The generator existed (ADR-160) but was both mislabeled and invisible for this case: the `api`-scoped button lives under User Settings → **Save from the web** as a "clipper token," and every minter renders as nothing until its signing secret is set — and Tyler's Vercel instance had no `LEDGR_*` auth secrets at all. Adding a **third purpose** rather than widening either existing one.

- **`LEDGR_APP_SECRET`** — a third signing secret. App tokens are `access`/`api`, the same shape as clipper tokens, so the four `api` routes needed **no change**. Rotating it revokes every app token and leaves MCP, the phone connector, and the clipper alone. That isolation is why it isn't just a reuse of the clipper secret: otherwise revoking Overtone would kill the web clipper.
- **Tokens are named.** `signAppToken(sub, label)` embeds an optional `lbl` (the same lowercase/digits/hyphens rule as `make-token.mjs`), so `verifyApiToken` reports the caller as `app:overtone` in machine-route logs instead of an anonymous `api` caller. Additive: pre-ADR-179 tokens verify unchanged and an unlabeled one reports a bare `app`. **The label identifies, it does not revoke** — worth keeping those separate in your head.
- **Build → API Tokens (`/build/api`)**, its own MAINTAIN sidebar entry. Type the app name, Generate, copy once. The page also lists the four endpoints an `api` token opens, a working `curl`, the revocation semantics, and a live status row for all three credential paths (app / clipper / static env) — so "why is there no button" is answerable from the page itself, which is the failure this whole slice came from.
- **Still no token list and no individual revoke** — the ADR-160 tradeoff is unchanged and now stated on the page. A DB-backed `api_tokens` table would give both, but it's a `schema.md` change and would make the pure/sync verifiers async across ~20 routes. Left as the known upgrade path.
- **Verified:** `verify-minted-tokens.mts` extended (assertions 6–10: roundtrip, label round-trip, caller naming, isolation both directions, **app-secret rotation killing app tokens while a clipper token survives**, label validation, unlabeled fallback) — all pass. Live against a running dev server with the real secret: `POST /api/machine/items` with a minted token → **201**, bogus → **401**, none → **401** (test item soft-deleted after). `/build/api` renders 200 with the input + Generate button in the DOM. `tsc` + `eslint` clean.
- **Owed:** deploy (`LEDGR_APP_SECRET` is already set in Vercel production, so the page goes live with the next deploy — until then the button only works locally); a COLLAB heads-up to Brandon, since this touches the shared auth contract and adds a Build sidebar entry he'll see. No schema, no migration, no new dependency.

## ✅ Recently done — Outline editing: Backspace deletes the line, empty bullets survive, Undo on the toolbar (2026-08-01, branch `fix/editor-outdent-empty-bullets-undo`, ADR-177)

Three complaints from a session of outline work in the "Don't Worry" manuscript.

- **Backspace at the start of a bullet now deletes the line break** instead of outdenting (`ListBackspaceJoin`, priority over ListKeymap). One press merges the line into the line above; when the line was the first item of its list, its sub-bullets are promoted into the gap it left rather than being stranded under an empty placeholder bullet (Brandon: *"if the line is empty it should go away and drag the sub bullets over, but it should NOT outdent before the line goes away"*). Otherwise the children keep their depth. Outdenting keeps Shift+Tab and the toolbar button. A selection still deletes normally, and `undoInputRule` still undoes a just-typed bullet. **The outdent complaint was a symptom of this** — reproduced in the browser, the button/Shift+Tab/Backspace all ran the same standard lift, and a sub-bullet moving with its parent is forced by markdown (a nested item is *defined* relative to its parent); what Brandon was actually doing was deleting a line the long way.
- **The "renders like a heading and I can't click into it" bullets were an empty-bullet parse trap, not corrupt data.** An empty `- ` flush under a paragraph is a **setext heading underline** to CommonMark, so the line above became an `<h2>` and the bullets vanished into it — markdown-it (every render/print/share/export) and marked (the editor) both agree. Fixed at both ends of the markdown manager: a blank line on serialize (the only unambiguous spelling), a zero-width sentinel on parse that never reaches the body (marked won't read an empty *first* nested item any other way). Old bodies heal on load and on render; nothing is rewritten until Brandon next edits that note.
- **Undo / Redo buttons lead the toolbar** (desktop and mobile — leftmost is the one spot that stays visible on a phone). ~100 grouped steps of history were already there from StarterKit; this exposes them. One settings checkbox hides the pair.
- Verified in a real browser at each step: the manuscript's broken section loads as clickable bullets, the round-trip is stable, and Backspace merges in one press across 11 list shapes (nested / flat / second-child / deep leaf / empty placeholder / empty-with-children / first item after a paragraph / first item of the document / checklist), with selection-delete and Shift+Tab unchanged and undo/redo walking edits back and forward with correct disabled states. `npx tsx scripts/verify-list-markdown.mts` (18 checks) + `tsc` + `eslint` clean.

## ✅ Recently done — Presentation Export: a clean copy + a slides document from one manuscript (2026-07-31, branch `sermon-presentation-export`, ADR-176)

Brandon wanted the Sunday hand-off: one press gives the sound booth a stripped black-and-white manuscript for print/PDF *and* a separate document holding the passages and quotes going on the screen.

- **The slide marker is a dedicated SLIDE MARK** (`<ins class="slide">`), a blue rule bracketing each end of the span, with a toolbar button left of the comment bubble. It started as a blue highlight and then a blue underline; both were rejected in practice, see the ADR-176 addendum: a color-encoded marker fights the text colors it sits on, and the underline channel already belongs to comments and `++underline++`. The signal is a SHAPE, so it reads on any color and coexists with a comment and a highlight on the same words. Highlights are ordinary highlighting again in all nine colors. Chosen after reading a real manuscript ("Don't Worry"), where the text-color channel is already carrying hand-made semantics (red = Scripture, pink = sticky statement, blue = key insight, green = transitions/illustrations). Brandon's blocking constraint — *"if I highlight a verse it won't be red anymore"* — is solved by the channel itself: background and foreground are orthogonal marks. Verified in both nesting orders in a real browser.
- **`mark { color: inherit }` was the bug behind that constraint**, and it was real: the UA stylesheet gives `<mark>` its own black `color`, which beats an inherited one, so highlighting colored text destroyed the color on every surface. Fixed in `globals.css` **and** print-html's `HL_CSS` (two separate stylesheets, both needed).
- **One pass, two outputs** (`booth-export.ts`), so the `**[SLIDE N]**` cue in the manuscript and slide N in the slides document can never drift. Bridging reuses ADR-174's rule: a highlight dragged across bullets is one slide, one cue.
- **Strip list:** colors flattened, `~~struck~~` material gone content and all, `++underline++` unwrapped, CriticMarkup notes dropped (already the print default). Bold/italic survive. A line whose only content was struck through is dropped, not left as an empty bullet.
- **Slides land in one child note, rewritten per run** (`POST /api/items/[id]/slides`); `revisions` keeps the prior version. No new table, column, or pointer.
- **Verified end to end** on a fixture with all six cases: cues numbered 1–3 with the two bridged bullets collapsing to one, colors and struck line gone, `++` unwrapped, comment note stripped, re-run updates the same note (`created: false`), and the button hydrates and fires from the shared footer. Verify script + `tsc` + `eslint` clean. Dev fixture removed after.
- **⚠️ Worth a word to Tyler before merge** (COLLAB.md updated): no schema and no syntax change, but it assigns a workflow meaning to a body-format primitive — the same posture ADR-174 took.
- **Separate bug, NOT fixed, Brandon's call:** `++underline++` renders as **literal plus signs** in the print/PDF/share/export render — markdown-it has no `++` rule, and the editor's underline mark serializes to it. The presentation copy unwraps it, so only *his own* PDFs show the `++`. Fixing it means one text pass in `markdown-render.ts`'s `prepare()` (or a `<u>` rule); left alone as out of scope.

## ✅ Recently done — Person connections unified on the generic edge (2026-07-30, branch `claude/person-connection-inconsistency-81387e`, ADR-175)

Brandon's screenshot showed a task with Kent and Roger in Linked Here while the PROPERTIES "People" row sat empty. Root cause: the user-created `people` typed field read only role=`people` edges, while every automatic writer (inbox chip, MCP `relateTo`, promoted action items) writes the default `related`. Fixed by making the odd surface conform: "is a person connected?" = any confirmed person edge, either direction, any role.

- **Migration `0049`** folds role=`people` edges into `related` and drops the field from the task type schema (idempotent; no-op for an instance without the field). **Run `db:migrate` before deploying the code.**
- **`PeopleRow`** (new, task rail): role-blind read, edits via `RelationField`'s new null-role mode (add = default `related` edge, remove = role-blind), mention-only persons as read-only `@` chips.
- **`RelatedPanel claimPersons`**: the rail's row owns confirmed persons so they don't repeat below (same claiming events do); suggested person edges stay in the panel for confirm/reject.
- **Inbox/Triage People chip** now shows linked first names in the filled style (`relatedSummaryFor` grew a SQL-side `type` filter); still add-only.
- Not touched: event `attending`, group `members`, hiring `reference`, and the `relation` property kind itself.

## ✅ Recently done — Comment hover fixed, and the card is edited in place (2026-07-30, branch `claude/comment-hover-inplace-edit`, ADR-174a)

Three symptoms Brandon hit on the ADR-174 build, one cause: **the hover highlight set a class on ProseMirror's own DOM.** PM's DOMObserver treats an attribute change on a mark span as a foreign mutation, re-reads the range and redraws the span; the redraw replaces the element under the pointer, `mouseover` fires on the new one, and it loops. Measured at ~60 redraws a second while the pointer just rested on a commented word. That thrash swallowed the highlight, the card's click, and click-to-place-caret inside commented text (arrow keys still worked, which was the tell that only the pointer path was broken).

- **Hover is now plugin state plus `Decoration.inline` over the comment's range.** `setLit` returns early when the hovered comment is unchanged, which is what makes a loop impossible: PM's own redraw re-fires `mouseover`, so the repeat must be a no-op. A meta-only transaction changes neither doc nor selection, so no save and no React re-render.
- **The card keeps a direct class toggle**, because widget decorations are exempt from the observer. Rebuilding the widget for hover state was rejected: a tap has no hover frame before the press, so it would swallow taps on mobile.
- **The CSS has three selectors on purpose.** The read view puts both classes on one span; the editor's decoration nests PM's span INSIDE the mark's, so that case re-declares the underline and paints over the parent's rather than recoloring it.
- **In-place editing (Brandon: "the note should just BECOME editable in place").** The card now hides while its own note is open (`visibility: hidden`, so the float keeps its space and nothing reflows), which turns ADR-173's card-positioned panel into "the note became a field" instead of a panel covering it.
- **Verified in a real visible browser this time** at 1280px and 375px: hover lights every line of a bridged comment plus its card, **0 DOM mutations in a second of resting hover**, card click opens the field in place with focus in the textarea, Escape restores the card, Save rewrites both segments, and a click inside commented text lands the caret at that offset. The lesson worth keeping: a hidden Browser pane freezes transitions and ResizeObservers, so the synchronous DOM checks that passed for ADR-174 could never have caught this.

## ✅ Recently done — Comments bridge multiple lines (2026-07-30, branch `claude/multiline-comment-bridging-994f76`, ADR-174)

A comment can now annotate text that runs across paragraphs, list items, headings, or a soft break and stay ONE comment: one margin card, one outline row, one edit, one delete, one hover group.

- **The markdown does not change, and must not.** CriticMarkup is inline, so a pair cannot cross a block boundary; the canonical form is **one pair per line sharing one note**, and the note text is the comment's identity. Tiptap already serialized it that way on its own (it closes and reopens an inline mark at every block boundary and hard break). `comment-markdown.ts`'s old ponytail note suggested widening the regex to `[\s\S]` — that note now says the opposite and why: invalid nesting across a `<p>` break, no round-trip through the mark, and an unclosed `{>>` free to swallow the document.
- **The real bug behind "editing one didn't update the others":** `extendMarkRange` → `getMarkRange` walks `$pos.parent`, so it is bounded to one textblock. Replaced by `commentRangeAt`, a run walk over the doc where block nodes and hard breaks never end a run. The toolbar path also now carries the caret as `at`, which it never did — with a caret (empty selection) inside a comment, Save used to set a stored mark instead of rewriting the run.
- **Hover across blocks is one class, not more CSS.** `+`/`:has()` cannot reach a segment in another paragraph, so `comment-hover.ts` toggles `.cmt-lit` on everything sharing the comment's `data-note` — the attribute the mark already rendered, now emitted by `renderComments` too, so one helper serves the editor plugin and the read view. The `:hover` rules remain as the no-JS baseline.
- **Accepted ceiling:** two different comments with byte-identical notes and only structure between them merge. The upgrade if it bites is an id, not a wider match.
- **Verified live** (editor + read view): paragraph → heading → paragraph selection = 3 segments / 1 card / 3 pairs saved; edit rewrites every segment and persists; Delete keeps the prose; hover lights the whole group; the outline yields 3 rows for 8 segments. 54 verify checks (the pure parser plus the editor's range walk on a headless doc), `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean.
- **Worth a word to Tyler** before merge: rendering/grouping only, no schema and no syntax change, but it sits near the canonical-body-format line.

## ✅ Recently done — Comment UI rework + comments in the outline (2026-07-30, branch `comments-ui-rework`, ADR-173)

Two halves in one PR, both on ADR-170's comments. **Half A** was written in an earlier session, never committed, and parked as a patch in OneDrive `Claude\_Inbox\Ledgr - Comments UI handoff\` — restored from there and rendered in a browser for the first time here. **Half B** was the planned follow-on.

- **The four fixes were four different root causes**, not one styling pass: `.cmt-note` *inherited* size and weight from what it annotated (a comment on an `h2` was a second headline) → resets inherited text properties, sizes in `rem`, surface-1 → surface-2; the editor row lived next to the sticky toolbar and its autofocused textarea scrolled the document to the top → a **popover positioned in the host wrapper's coordinate space**, so it scrolls with the note; the open handler sat on the anchored **text**, so commented text could never take a caret → deleted, only the card/icon opens it; and the mobile `=` pin was an `::after` **pseudo-element**, therefore un-hit-testable, which is *why* the whole phrase was the tap target → `.cmt-note` itself collapses into a tappable bubble, fixing glyph and tap target together.
- **Clicking away from the popover SAVES a non-empty draft.** It floats over a document you keep clicking, so discarding on a stray click would be a trap. Escape and ✕ discard. Deliberate asymmetry.
- **Two locked outline decisions (Brandon, against mockups):** two-line rows (note, then anchored text), and **no comment ticks on the collapsed rail**. **Open question answered: keep `MIN_HEADINGS = 2` as-is** — a note with comments but <2 headings gets no list, and that's accepted; its comments stay reachable in the margin and on their inline bubbles.
- **The build plan carried a bug, caught only in the browser.** Its anchor walk tested `previousElementSibling.classList.contains("cmt")`, but a nested mark **inverts the nesting** (`<strong><span class="cmt">bold</span></strong>`), so the walk stopped at the `<strong>` and the row showed "inside it" instead of "a run with bold inside it". Now accepts an element that *is* `.cmt` **or contains one**; the run is self-bounding because an earlier comment's `.cmt-note` sits between and holds no `.cmt`.
- **Two pre-existing gaps found in passing, deliberately left:** the Tiptap editor doesn't parse a **point comment** (`{>>note<<}` alone) — it stays literal text there while the read view renders `.cmt-point`, so the editor's list is short by one on such a note; and the read view renders `##` as `<h3>`, pushing `###` out of the outline's 1–3 scan.
- **Browser-verified at 1280px and 375px, editor and read view** — the point of the session, since half A had never been rendered even once. Popover lands on the card and scrolls with it (−200 against −200), no viewport jump, caret goes into commented text, mobile bubble is a 22×18 tap target, section renders in pinned/flyout/sheet and not on the rail, jump lands 16px below the sticky chrome, rows track edits live, no-comment notes show no divider. `tsc`, `eslint`, `next build` clean.

## ✅ Recently done — Fuzzy search: stacked guesses with per-criterion confidence (2026-07-29, branch `feat/fuzzy-search`, ADR-172)

> **🟢 Follow-up 2 (2026-07-30) — quick search escalates into the tuned page.** The palette now has a **permanent footer button**: "Advanced search for <query>" (an explanatory line when nothing is typed), carrying the query across via `?q=`. It replaces a path that existed but was invisible — Enter with a query and *zero* results already pushed `/search?q=`. The PAGES row from follow-up 1 was removed: a permanent footer makes it a second, worse door that competes for arrow-key selection and drops the query. **Mobile quick search was never lost** (`mobileTrailingControls` opens the palette); verified at 375px along with the whole escalation flow.

> **🔴 Follow-up (2026-07-30) — the search PAGE had no door.** `NavShell`/`Launcher` hardcoded `href === "/search"` into "open the command palette," `command-index.ts` deliberately omitted Search, and the picker offered one "Search" entry that became the palette. Net effect: **ADR-172 shipped to prod reachable only by typing the URL.** The hijack is deleted — a `/search` slot now navigates like any destination. The palette loses nothing: it keeps the global ⌘K plus a permanent hardcoded Search button in all four layouts, which is exactly why the slot was redundant. Picker entry relabelled **"Advanced search"**, and the palette now lists it as a jump target. Brandon's nav has no `/search` slot, so **his door is ⌘K → "Advanced search"** until he adds the slot in Build → Navigation. Fixed in passing: `verify-nav-and-deletes`'s `tools children cap at 20` was red on `main` and unpassable (fed 10, asserted 20).


Search for the thing you **half-remember**. Pile up whatever you recall (a word, a rough date, a hunch about the type), set how sure you are about **each piece separately**, and results rank by the weighted total instead of being filtered down to nothing. No AI (Principle 3), no schema change, no new production dependency. Plan: `plans/fuzzy-search.html` (local artifact, gitignored). Check: `npx tsx scripts/verify-search-deep.mts`.

- **The rule that shapes everything:** confidence means two different things. A **date is continuous**, so the dial bends how sharply its score falls off and is **never** a hard filter at any stop — even at Sure, an out-of-window item is ranked down, not dropped, so a strong word match can still surface it. A **type / term / relation / tag is categorical**, so `sure` *is* a real `WHERE` filter (today's behavior) and the lower stops boost without excluding. Don't "unify" these; the asymmetry is the design.
- **`src/lib/search.ts` is untouched, deliberately.** Quick search, the command palette, the @ typeahead and MCP `search_items` all keep the exact path bit-for-bit. Fuzzy mode engages only on a `term=`/`tag=`/`when=` param or a `~confidence` suffix; a suffix-less `type=note` still means a hard filter. Keep it that way — the whole feature is revertible by deleting files.
- **The synonym map must never reach the browser.** `src/data/synonyms.json` is ~1.8MB of WordNet, imported only by `src/lib/synonyms.ts`, which is server-only. That's why the "+N similar" hint comes back **in the API response** instead of being computed client-side, and why `parseFuzzyWhen` keeps its own tiny 1–12 number map instead of importing from `synonyms.ts` (`nl-date.ts` ships to the client with task capture). Verified: the map appears in `.next/server`, never in `.next/static`. **Don't import `synonyms.ts` from a client component.**
- **Regenerating synonyms:** `npx tsx scripts/build-synonyms.mts` downloads WordNet 3.1, prunes it (3 senses/word, 8 synonyms/word, single lowercase words only) and rewrites the committed JSON. Dev-only, never runs in production. The pruning is the quality lever — WordNet lists senses most-frequent-first, so cutting the tail is what keeps "lesson" from dragging in the synonyms of "moral of the story".
- **WordNet does not know Edgewood.** It will not link *teaching* → *preaching*. That's expected, and the personal dictionary in User Settings ("Search dictionary") is the fix; it wins over WordNet and takes effect with no redeploy. It starts empty on purpose — add a line only when a search actually misses.
- **Two bugs worth remembering.** (1) A bound parameter in date arithmetic **must be cast**: `$1::date - $2` made Postgres resolve `-` to `date - date → integer`, failing as `date >= integer`. Every JS number in the SQL now carries `::int`/`::numeric`. `.toSQL()` cannot catch this class of bug — only a live query can. (2) `deepSearchQuery` now drops a date criterion with no usable range rather than trusting the caller, because criteria come from URL params where an unparseable phrase yields nothing.
- **Verified live** against the ~9k-item dev corpus: 83–178ms warm; a Sure "within the last week" still returns month-old term matches (ranked down, date contributing ~0.00); soft `type=meeting` leaves task/transcript/event hits in place; `created` vs `updated` genuinely diverge (1 item made last week vs 4 worked on). `tsc` + `next build` clean, verify script ALL PASS.
- **Browser-verified** on the dev-auth preview (1110px): the panel opens from Tune with the plain view untouched behind it; a word row shows its live "+6 similar" count and returns 50 ranked matches with why-chips; a tag row (Folder = inbox) narrows to the tagged emails; picking Roger + "linked as Attending" cuts 50 matches to 34, all events he attended, chipped "Roger Knowlton (Attending)". The soft-criterion promise is visible in the result list: with the tag at Might, an event with no Folder tag still appears, ranked below the tagged rows. Request serialization confirmed via the network log (`term=teaching~probably`, `tag=folder:inbox~might`, `role=attending`).
- **The criterion rows are populated from the live type registry**, deduped by property key across types: date-kind fields join the When source select (Date, Received at, Logos Created/Modified), select/multi_select fields become tag rows (Folder, Kind, Medium, Horizon, Stage…), relation-kind fields become the "linked as" roles (Tags, Project, People, Attending, Members, Reference). A field with no options is filtered out so no row is a dead end, and each group hides entirely when the workspace has none.
- **Deferred by hiding, not deleting:** number/text/checkbox/url property criteria. The `Criterion` union and the URL vocabulary already generalize to them; only the pickers are missing. Add when a real search misses for want of one. Note the two plain date inputs are hidden while tuning — the When box accepts an exact date too, so they'd be redundant there; the `from`/`to` params still work in fuzzy mode for back-compat with a bookmarked URL.

## ✅ Recently done — Dashboards as activity surfaces (2026-07-29, branch `dashboard-activity`, ADR-171)

Brandon redirected the feature: **a dashboard is where you run an activity** (today, planning, organizing), not a read-only arrangement of cards. Nine slices, one commit each, built by parallel agents on file-disjoint lanes. The engine (saved views, widgets as tolerant jsonb, one PATCH path, the shared resolver) was not touched. Plan: `plans/dashboard-phase2-mcp-parity-host-widgets.html` (local artifact, gitignored); audit: `explorations/dashboard-overhaul.md`.

- **Shipped separately first, and deployed:** the blank-dashboard fix (PR #226). `measureBeforeMount` on RGL 1.5.3's WidthProvider latched `width: 0` because its ResizeObserver stayed on the detached pre-mount placeholder. **Every dashboard, including Home, rendered blank in production from 2026-07-11 to 2026-07-29.** There is a DO-NOT-RESTORE comment in `RglInner.tsx`; leave it.
- **KILLED, on Brandon's call:** host-scoped widgets on item canvases (the old `dashboard-widgets.md` carry-forward). The record engine already serves it: open a person, their tasks and notes are at the bottom. Do not rebuild this.
- **Focus is hidden, not deleted** (W4). `applyFocus`, `focus_item_id`, the parser, the resolver and `FocusPicker.tsx` all still work. The pill now renders in BOTH modes because its ✕ was the only way to clear an existing focus, and the picker that held it is gone.
- **The interactive gate is the `today` prop.** Set → rows get the ADR-142 row menu, view widgets get the inline add, board widgets get card drag. Absent (the Desk's read-only panel) → all plain. Use the same gate for anything new.
- **Two cross-cutting fixes came out of this and matter elsewhere:** (1) `RowMenu` now portals its menu, because an RGL cell's `transform` becomes the containing block for `position: fixed` — that also fixed the ADR-069 item canvas, which had the same latent bug. (2) The dashboard resolver now carries the type's `statuses` and `groupPropKind`, so a board widget reproduces `/views/[id]`'s drag guard exactly; without it a status-board drop wrote a status the type never defined.
- **Inline add inherits deterministically or not at all** (Principle 3): the type always, and a date only on an explicit today window, on whichever column the filter actually windows on. A `focusedToday` view also gets the focus day-stamp. On a focused board the new item is related to the focus item via the existing create-then-relate pair, since `POST /api/items` cannot relate in one call.
- **Verified live** on the dev-auth preview: complete + undo from a widget row, inline add landing in its view (list, stat, Recently Touched and Agenda all updating), repointing a widget's view from the gear without losing size or position, collapse holding in edit mode, and a two-row edit header at 375px. `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean; `verify-dashboards` / `verify-dashboard-canvas` / `verify-mcp` / `verify-views` all ALL PASS.
- **Known and deliberate:** an empty widget that carries an inline add does NOT fold (capture beats compactness) so it keeps full height; a header-off `view` with rows gets no tile link (an outer `<a>` would hijack row clicks); Background stayed a sibling of the ⋯ menu because `FloatingMenu` closes on any click outside its own ref and `BackgroundPanel` is a sibling portal; container tab persistence is built but was not browser-verified (no container widget exists in the dev data); `verify-mcp`'s always-on tool list had been stale and red on `main`, fixed in passing.

## ✅ Recently done — Comments on the body (2026-07-28, branch `feat/md-comments`, ADR-170)

Select a span of a note and leave a comment. It lives **in the markdown** as CriticMarkup, `{==anchored text==}{>>note<<}` (or `{>>note<<}` alone), so it degrades legibly in any other editor and the exported `.md` keeps it. No table and no migration: the anchor *is* the text, so comments can't drift or orphan, and revisions / backup / OneDrive export cover them for free. Core change, Tyler cleared it.

- **Where the logic lives:** `src/lib/editor/comment-markdown.ts` (the pure parser, both ends: expand or strip) and `src/components/markdown-editor/comment-mark.ts` (the Tiptap mark + the margin-card decoration). Check: `npx tsx scripts/verify-comment-markdown.mts`.
- **The visual rule, don't break it:** a **comment owns the underline channel, a highlight owns the fill channel.** That's what lets a commented phrase sit inside a yellow highlight and read as two things. Never give `.cmt` a permanent background; hover borrows a fill and gives it back.
- **Comments never reach a reader.** `markdownToHtml` renders them by default (read view + FTS), and print / PDF / pinned-offline / share / paper `.docx` all pass `comments: false`. `?comments=1` opts a print in; a share link can't opt in at all. **If you add another human-facing render path, opt it out.**
- **The trap:** the `comment` mark must stay **OUT of `HTML_WRAPPED_MARKS`** (extensions.ts). It emits CriticMarkup, not raw HTML, so its content needs the serializer's normal escaping. Adding it there "for consistency" breaks bold inside commented text.
- **Clicks are DOM-level on purpose.** ProseMirror's `handleClick` silently never matches: the mark is `inclusive: false` and the card is a widget at the run's end, so a position lookup there finds no mark. Edit paths carry an explicit position and do `setTextSelection(at).extendMarkRange("comment")`.
- Verified live: 3 rich⇌source flips byte-identical with zero backslashes (including a comment inside a highlight and an anchor containing `*emphasis*`), create-from-toolbar / click-to-edit / delete all correct, print clean, margin cards stacking with no overlap.
- **Deferred, by hiding not deleting:** a per-share-link "include comments" toggle (plumbing is already in, only the share-dialog UI is missing), an atom node so a hand-written point comment is editable as a chip rather than literal text, and timestamps (no metadata slot in-body; an optional date prefix is the upgrade path).

## ✅ Recently done — Date pickers commit on confirm (2026-07-28, ADR-169)

Brandon: scheduling from the Inbox saved a date he never picked the moment he moved the browser's calendar to another month, and the popup closed on the way out. Cause: the "Pick" field wrote on the *first* change event, and a native date input fires one while the user is still navigating inside the picker.

- New `src/components/ui/DateInput.tsx` keeps a **draft** and commits on "Set" (or Enter). Its Set button `preventDefault`s mousedown so focus stays on the input — a surface that exits on blur can't unmount before the click lands.
- Swapped into every date field that wrote-and-vanished: `InboxTaskControls`, `RowMenu`, `DayField` (rail Schedule/Due), `SubtaskSchedule`, `AddTaskCard`. Today / Tomorrow / +1 wk / Clear stay one click.
- **Use `DateInput` for any new date field inside a popup or menu.** Inline field editors that stay on screen (`FieldStrip`, `CustomProperties`, `RelatedRow`) still write on change on purpose.
- Verified live by replaying the picker's own event (set value + dispatch `input`) on all four surfaces: no PATCH, popup stays open, Set appears; commit path confirmed end to end including the subtask relative offset.

## ✅ Recently done — Item page: sticky chrome row + one shared sticky offset (2026-07-28, ADR-168)

The trash / ⋯ / word-count row is the item page's only chrome for those actions, and a long note scrolled it away. It's now `sm:sticky` at z-35, and `ItemCanvas` publishes its height as **`--item-chrome-h`** on the `[data-toc-scope]` element so the canvas's other two sticky layers (the editor's formatting bar at z-30, the ADR-167 outline at z-40) pin *under* it via `calc(var(--nav-pt) + var(--item-chrome-h))` instead of sliding over it. The variable is set only where the sticky row renders, so the modal, Desk panels, and mobile fall back to `0px` untouched.

- **If you add another sticky layer to the item canvas, pin it at that `calc()`, not at `--nav-pt` alone.**
- **Trap fixed in passing:** `FloatingToc` read its offsets with `parseFloat(getComputedStyle(scope).getPropertyValue("--nav-pt"))`, which returns the authored token — so `"3.5rem"` parsed as **3.5px**. Its jump offset had been ~52px short all along, latent because it landed close enough. `stickyTopOf()` now reads the sticky layer's own computed `top` (a used length, always px). Don't reintroduce `parseFloat` on a custom property.
- Same commit: `max-w-full` on `CustomProperties`' inputs and `RelationField`'s typeahead + dropdown, with the dropdown's anchor moved to the whole field. The task rail scrolls on `overflow-y` (which makes `overflow-x` auto too), so a few px of spill from a `w-56`/`w-64` control became a scrollbar that shifted the rail's labels out of view.
- `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean; offsets confirmed live via computed styles (row height 52.8px = outline's resolved `top`).
- **Verified by eye** on a 29k-char note (2026-07-28): scrolled, the three layers stack contiguously at `0 / 52.8 / 96.8` — chrome row z-35, outline pinned at its bottom edge z-40, formatting bar below it z-30, no gap and no overlap. Modal, Desk panel, and mobile all read `--item-chrome-h` as unset → every sticky layer still pins at `0`, i.e. unchanged. TOC jump lands the heading at `stickyTopOf() + 16` = 69px, confirming the `parseFloat` fix.
- **Known shortfall, pre-existing, left alone (Brandon's call, 2026-07-28):** the TOC jump offset counts the sticky chrome row but *not* the editor's 44px formatting bar sitting below it, so in rich-edit mode a jumped-to heading still lands ~28px behind that bar. Predates ADR-168 (the outline never accounted for the editor bar) and this change made it strictly better, not worse. The fix, if it's ever wanted, is one more term in `FloatingToc`'s `offsetRef` — measure the bar rather than hardcoding 44px.

## ✅ Recently done — Table of contents: container-relative + per-item pinned sidebar (2026-07-27, branch `feat/toc-container-relative-pin`, ADR-167)

The outline was missing in the Desk. Root cause: `FloatingToc` mounts only in `ItemCanvas`, which the Desk bypasses — and it positioned itself `fixed` to the viewport, so N panels would have stacked N rails on one screen edge anyway. Reworked to a `sticky` layer inside its `[data-toc-scope]` (must be the scope's **first child**), with the presentation chosen from the container's measured size instead of a viewport media query. Added Brandon's third state: hover/click the marks → flyout → **pin** → a drag-resizable right sidebar the content makes room for, remembered **per item** in `users.settings.tocPinnedItems`.

- Dropped the `variant: "page" | "modal"` prop; as a side effect the **peek panel gained the rail it never had**.
- Pin is per item and synced (it's a fact about the note); rail width is per device (`ledgr:toc-width`, it's about the screen). Not `items.properties` — that would bump `updated_at` and restamp the note as edited.
- `canPin` takes container width **and** viewport height, because a landscape phone is wider than a portrait tablet: no pin on mobile, yes on tablet.
- Verified live: two split Desk panels holding two different notes render two independent outlines with independent pins; clamps, persistence, reload, narrow-panel fallback, and unpin all confirmed. `tsc` + `eslint` + `next build` clean.
- **Deferred:** per-*type* pinning as a Build setting (worth seeing if it's wanted after living with per-item).

**Same chunk — live editing context now works in the Desk (ADR-167a).** Same root cause: `ActiveContextTracker` mounted only in `ItemCanvas`. The Desk now mounts **one** reporter for the whole surface (`DeskActiveContext`), pointed at the focused panel's active tab, since only one panel holds the pen. Selections are scoped to `[data-desk-focused] [data-toc-scope]`, resolved per selection change, so a highlight in a follower panel isn't attributed to the focused note.

- **Fixed a latent race while in there:** the tracker used to DELETE in the same cleanup that preceded the next item's POST. Rare page-to-page, but Desk focus switching would have hit it constantly and randomly blanked the context. Teardown is now a mount-only effect, and the DELETE carries `?itemId=` (`clearActiveContext(ownerId, onlyItemId?)`) so a straggler can't wipe fresh context.
- **Known gap:** a template prototype opened in a Desk panel still reports context (the canvas suppresses it via `item.isTemplate`; the Desk doc store doesn't carry that field). Harmless, not worth a store field yet.
- **Verification note for next time:** `liveContextEnabled` is **off** on the dev branch and `noteEditingPromptItemId` is null, so flipping it through `PATCH /api/settings` would *create* a "Note Editing Partner" prompt item. To test, flip the flag directly in the DB instead and restore it after.

## ⟢ IN PROGRESS — Planner → app-wide scheduling surface (2026-07-23, branch `worktree-planner-timeline`, ADR-166)

Rebuilding the tasks-only Planner into a Ledgr-wide feature: any dated item renders on Month / Multi-day / a new **Timeline** mode; writable dates drag, start+end resizes, missing capabilities just drop. See `plans/timeline-view.html` (local visual plan, gitignored) for the full design.

- **✅ Slice 2 (foundation) done** (commit `c88ad40`): `src/lib/placement.ts` (the `resolvePlacement`/`buildPatch` seam) + `scripts/verify-placement.mts` (29 assertions PASS); `items.end_at` column (migration `0048`, applied to dev); pure zone helpers extracted to `src/lib/zone.ts`; `PropertyDef.withEnd` + the `properties[key__end]` range convention; `endAt` accepted through item-input/mutations; `ViewItem` gains `endAt`/`noteDate`.
- **✅ Slice 3 (Timeline read-only) done** (commit `db20e89`): `src/lib/timeline-geometry.ts` (+ verify, 21 PASS) + `src/components/planner/PlannerTimeline.tsx` — horizontal zoomable axis (Hour→5-Year), chips/bars, lane-packing, ruler, today line, no-date rail, in-component zoom switch. Third "Timeline" button in `PlannerCalendar`; `tz` threaded from ViewRenderer. **Verified live** (dev-auth preview, 200-item calendar view): mode toggle, all zooms, ruler, today line, lane-packed chips, no errors.
- **⛔ Slice 1 (Multi-day over-scroll) SKIPPED** (Brandon, 2026-07-23): measured the grid — it ends cleanly at midnight (scrollHeight 1248 = 40+56+1152), no phantom space; the only "issue" is a full-24h render leaving out-of-work-hours dead scroll. Since the Timeline (horizontal, no vertical dead space) replaces Multi-day in slice 5, not worth fixing the retiring component.
- **✅ Slice 4 (Timeline drag) done** (2026-07-23): `PlannerTimeline` is now writable. New pure drag math in `timeline-geometry.ts` — `applyTimelineDrag(kind, origin, start, end, deltaPx, zoom)` (span-preserving move; front/back edge resizes; snap- and precision-aware so a day-only field moves in whole days, a timed field snaps to the slot at fine zoom or moves whole-days-keeping-time at coarse) + 16 new assertions in `verify-timeline-geometry.mts` (37 PASS total). One Pointer-Events path serves mouse + touch (`touch-action:none` + guarded `setPointerCapture`, 4px move threshold before a press becomes a drag, else it opens the item); AbortController tears the per-drag window listeners down. Optimistic `override` + `buildPatch` PATCH + undo toast (mirrors `PlannerTimeGrid`); the axis window is computed from server-truth placements so a live drag never shifts the coordinate system. Edge auto-scroll near the viewport sides. **Verified live** (dev preview, 200-item tasks planner, PointerEvent-driven since the pane can't composite for screenshots): move a chip +1 day → single PATCH `scheduledDate` + optimistic reposition; back-edge resize of a scheduled→due bar → `dueDate` +1 day, start untouched, width grows; drop-to-rail → both dates cleared, item moves to rail; rail→axis → `scheduledDate` set to the drop day. All 200 ok, no console errors; test item restored to its original date.
- **✅ Slice 5 (Month refit + meetingAt gate + Multi-day retired) done** (2026-07-23): `PlannerMonth` now reads/writes through the placement layer (shared `deriveSpec` extracted to `placement.ts` with type-only view imports), not the hardcoded scheduled/due field — so events (meetingAt), notes (note_date), and custom date props place and drag alongside tasks; a day drop moves the start there (preserving time-of-day) and shifts any end by the same number of days; read-only anchors (created/updated) show but aren't draggable (`can.move` gates `draggable` + `data-card-id`). **The meetingAt gate:** `ViewRenderer`'s calendar branch now routes `meetingAt` (not just plan/scheduled/due) to the interactive `PlannerCalendar` (was static grid). **Multi-day retired (defer-by-hiding):** the "Multi-day" segment is gone from the toggle (only re-appears if a stored view's `display.mode==="timegrid"`, which still renders — `PlannerTimeGrid` is kept, unrouted, recoverable); the month "+N more"/day-number now opens the **Timeline** centered on that day (new `focusDay` prop). **Placement-seam bug fixed along the way (CORE):** a timed task with a `scheduledTime` block but no due date used to gain a spurious `dueDate` on any Timeline/Month drag (deriveSpec's `end=dueDate` + the block-derived end colliding in `buildPatch`); now the block-end is derived only when the spec has no end field, and `buildPatch` preserves the existing block duration on a plain move (verify-placement +4 assertions, all pass). **Verified live** (dev preview): Month task move fired one PATCH keeping `scheduledTime {10:30, 90}` and NOT setting dueDate; openDay switched to Timeline and centered off-today; a temp `meetingAt` calendar view mounted the interactive planner (Month+Timeline) instead of the static grid; fresh reload renders clean (the mid-edit HMR console errors were stale intermediate chunks). **Note:** `placeBy` ("Place by Due") is no longer read by Month/Timeline (parity; superseded by start/end field pickers in slice 6). **Minor follow-up:** on a meetingAt calendar, the month double-click still quick-creates a *task* (usePlannerCreate) — wrong type for an events view; worth type-awareness later.
- **✅ Slice 6 (builder controls + chip polish) done** (2026-07-23): **Coarse-zoom chip polish** — at month-zoom and out (ppd < 100) the Timeline packs items by their TRUE day-extent (not the 120px `CHIP_MIN` that made a 1-day item smear across weeks and inflate lanes); a chip becomes a compact 16px dot with the label overflowing to the right (`overflow-visible`). Verified live at Year zoom: chips render at 16px, labels overflow (scrollWidth 121 vs box 16), lane count reflects genuine same-day overlap (6) instead of the ~13-lane smear. **Builder controls** — `ViewBuilder` now has a calendar-display section (shown when layout=calendar): **Default view** (Month/Timeline; the retired "timegrid" is selectable only if the stored view already uses it, so editing round-trips it) and **Timeline zoom** (all 7 zooms, shown when mode=timeline). The existing **Date field** picker already serves as the start anchor; `deriveSpec`'s auto-pairing (meeting→end, scheduled→due) covers the end. Payload now carries `display` (preserving other display fields). Verified live: a calendar view's edit page renders both controls initialized from stored `display`, and create/round-trip persists `{mode, zoom}`. **Lens relabel** — the event list's bespoke "Timeline" lens is now labeled **"Agenda"** (internal `kind:"timeline"` unchanged, no migration) so it isn't confused with the planner's new Timeline calendar mode (`list-lenses.ts`, `ListTabsEditor.tsx`).
  - **⏭️ Remaining ADR-166 follow-ups (deferred — surface-specific, want Brandon's eye):** (1) **event-canvas End field** — add an `end_at` input to `EventCanvas` so events become resizable spans (today events without `end_at` render as chips; the resize path is exercised only by scheduled→due tasks + custom prop ranges). Highest-value follow-up. (2) **`withEnd` date-property toggle** — expose the `PropertyDef.withEnd` flag (slice 2) in the type property editor so a custom date property can carry its `__end` sibling range. (3) an **explicit start/end field picker** (beyond the auto-pairing) for ad-hoc pairings / custom-prop ranges. (4) minor: on a meetingAt calendar the month double-click still quick-creates a *task* (usePlannerCreate) — wrong type for an events view.

> **✅ RESOLVED — stale drizzle meta snapshots.** `db:generate` had been re-emitting already-applied objects (0041 `activity_events`/`home`, 0042 `next_action`/`composition`/`default_widgets`, 0046 `passage_refs`, 0047 `active_context`) as fresh CREATE/ADD statements, because the committed `drizzle/meta/*_snapshot.json` files didn't reflect those migrations (confirmed on HEAD `747ebb1`). Deploys were never affected (`db:migrate` replays SQL, ignores snapshots). **Fixed as a side effect of slice 2:** the auto-generated `0048_snapshot.json` captured the complete current schema, and keeping it (while trimming only the SQL to the one real `end_at` delta) re-baselined the diff source. Confirmed: `db:generate` now reports "No schema changes." The intermediate `0041`–`0047` snapshots stay internally inconsistent but are inert (Drizzle only diffs the latest snapshot), so no history rewrite is warranted.
